Auxiliary Results and Lemmas

Lemma 6
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
4
and
5
hold. Then:

(a) If
\left|(nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{2,i,t}^{2}-\varepsilon_{1,i,t}^{2}]\right|\succ G_{1}
,
then
\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{n,T}^{2\text{-side}}(p)]\rightarrow 1
, as
n,T\rightarrow\infty
.

(b) If
(nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{2,i,t}^{2}-\varepsilon_{1,i,t}^{2}]\succ G_{1}
, then
\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{n,T}^{1\text{-side}}(p)]\rightarrow 1
, as
n,T\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma 7
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
4
,
5
and
6
, we have
\omega_{n,T}^{2}=\sigma_{n,T}^{2}+\sigma_{U,n,T}^{2}+o(\omega_{n,T}^{2})
.

Lemma 8
.

We have
\hat{\sigma}_{U,n,T}^{2}\geq(2nT)^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\hat{\sigma}_{2,i}^{4}+n^{-3}\sum_{g=2}^{G_{1}}\sum_{g^{\prime}=1}^{g-1}(\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\hat{\sigma}_{2,i}^{2})(\sum_{i\in I_{g^{\prime}}}\hat{\sigma}_{2,i}^{2})
.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
,
4
,
5
and
6
hold. Then, under the null hypothesis,

\widehat{\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]}-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]=o_{p}(\omega_{n,T})\text{
\ \ \ and \ \ }\hat{\omega}_{n,T}^{2}/\omega_{n,T}^{2}=1+o_{p}(1).

(87)

Lemma 10
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
,
4
and
5
hold. Then
\hat{\omega}_{n,T}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
and
\widehat{\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]}=O_{p}(G_{1})
. Moreover,
\left|\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]\right|\leq KG_{1}
and
\omega_{n,T}^{2}\leq K
.

Appendix C
General Theory for Nonlinear Panel Likelihood Models

In this section, we present general results on the properties of estimators
for nonlinear panel models and derive the asymptotic distribution of the QLR
statistic for comparing different panel models. The proofs of these results
are provided in the Online Appendix of the paper.

C.1
General Framework

We consider a general nonlinear panel model with joint likelihood specified
in (
1
). Recall that
I_{g}
for
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
I_{m}
for
m\in\mathcal{M}
denote the partitions of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
and

\{1,\ldots,T\}
, respectively, induced by the group assignment functions
g(\cdot)
and
m(\cdot)
. For notational convenience, we assume that
m(\cdot)
is nondecreasing. This implies that for any two consecutive time
groups
m
and
m+1
, we have
\min I_{m+1}=\max I_{m}+1
.

Under this notation, the joint likelihood in (
1
) can be
written as

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi\left(z_{i,t};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right),

where
\psi\left(z_{i,t};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)\equiv\log f\left(z_{i,t};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)
. Let
(\theta^{\ast\top},\gamma_{g,m}^{\ast\top})^{\top}
denote the pseudo true value defined as

(\theta^{\ast\top},\gamma_{g,m}^{\ast\top})^{\top}=\underset{(\theta^{\top},\gamma_{g,m}^{\top})^{\top}\in\Phi}{\arg\max}\Psi_{g,m}(\theta,\gamma_{g,m}),

where
\Psi_{g,m}(\theta,\gamma_{g,m})\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(z_{i,t};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)\right]
,
\Phi
denotes the parameter space including
(\theta^{\ast\top},\gamma_{g,m}^{\ast\top})^{\top}
in its interior,
and
n_{g}
and
T_{m}
denote the sizes of
I_{g}
and
I_{m}

respectively. The unknown pseudo true parameters are estimated through
M-estimation, i.e.,

(\hat{\theta}^{\top},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},1}^{\top},\ldots,\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},M}^{\top})^{\top}\equiv\underset{\theta,\{\gamma_{\mathcal{G},m}\}_{m\in\mathcal{M}}}{\arg\max}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi\left(z_{i,t};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right),

where
\gamma_{\mathcal{G},m}=(\gamma_{g,m}^{\top})_{g\in\mathcal{G}}^{\top}
for
m=1,\ldots,M
. The M-estimator
(\hat{\theta}^{\top},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},1}^{\top},\ldots,\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},M}^{\top})^{\top}
satisfies the first-order conditions

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\frac{\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\hat{\theta},\hat{\gamma}_{g,m})}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0_{d_{\theta}\times 1},

(88)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\frac{\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\hat{\theta},\hat{\gamma}_{g,m})}{\partial\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0_{d_{\gamma}\times 1},

(89)

and for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
\ m\in\mathcal{M}
.

C.2
Asymptotic Theory of the M-Estimator

We establish the asymptotic properties of the M-estimator
(\hat{\theta}^{\top},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},1}^{\top},\ldots,\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},M}^{\top})^{\top}
, together with the asymptotic expansion of the
maximized joint likelihood in this subsection. We begin by stating
sufficient conditions under which these results hold.

Assumption 7
.

\min_{g\in\mathcal{G},m\in\mathcal{M}}\left[\Psi_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})-\sup_{\left\{\phi_{g,m}\in\Phi:\text{ }||\phi_{g,m}-\phi_{g,m}^{\ast}||>\eta\right\}}\Psi_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m})\right]>0
for each
\eta>0
, where
\Psi_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m})\equiv\Psi_{g,m}(\theta,\gamma_{g,m})
and
\phi_{g,m}\equiv(\theta^{\top},\gamma_{g,m}^{\top})^{\top}
.

Assumption 8
.

Let
\psi(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi})
be a function indexed
by the parameter
\tilde{\phi}=(\tilde{\phi}_{j})_{j\leq k}\in\mathop{\mathrm{i}nt}\Phi
, where
k\equiv\dim(\tilde{\phi})
and
\Phi
is a compact, convex
subset of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Let
\nu\equiv\left(\nu_{1},...,\nu_{k}\right)^{\top}
be a vector of non-negative integers,
\left|\nu\right|=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\nu_{j}
and
D^{\nu}\psi(z_{i,t},\tilde{\phi})=\partial^{\left|\nu\right|}\psi(z_{i,t},\tilde{\phi})/(\partial\tilde{\phi}_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\cdots\partial\tilde{\phi}_{k}^{\nu_{k}})
. Then: (i) there exists a function
M\left(z_{i,t}\right)
such
that

\left|D^{\nu}\psi(z_{i,t},\tilde{\phi}_{1})-D^{\nu}\psi(z_{i,t},\tilde{\phi}_{2})\right|\leq M\left(z_{i,t}\right)||\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{2}||

for all
\tilde{\phi}_{1},\tilde{\phi}_{2}\in\Phi
and
\left|v\right|\leq 4
; (ii) the function
M(z_{i,t})
satisfies
\sup_{i,t}\mathbb{E}[\left|M(z_{i,t})\right|^{p+\delta}]<K
for some
integer
p>\max\{4,k+\delta\}
and for some
\delta>0
; (iii) for all
multi-indices
v
with
\left|v\right|\leq 4
,
\max_{g\in\mathcal{G},m\in\mathcal{M}}\sup_{i,t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D^{\nu}\psi\left(z_{i,t},\phi_{g,m}^{\ast}\right)\right|^{4+\delta}\right]<K
where
\phi_{g,m}^{\ast}\equiv(\theta^{\ast\top},\gamma_{g,m}^{\ast\top})^{\top}
.

Let
\hat{\Psi}_{ab,g,m}\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{ab}(z_{i,t};\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})
and
\Psi_{ab,g,m}\equiv\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{ab,g,m}]
for
a,b\in\{\gamma,\theta\}
, where
\psi_{ab}(z_{i,t};\phi)\equiv\frac{\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi)}{\partial a\partial b^{\top}}
.

Assumption 9
.

The smallest eigenvalues of
-\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}

and
\Psi_{\theta\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\theta,g,m}-\Psi_{\theta\theta,g,m}
are bounded below by
K^{-1}

uniformly over
g
,
m
,
n
and
T
.

Assumption
7
is the identification uniqueness condition to ensure the
consistency of the M-estimator. Assumption
8
imposes smoothness and
uniform moment bounds on the quasi-likelihood function and its derivatives.
Assumption
9
provides local identification conditions for
\theta^{\ast}
and
\gamma_{g,m}^{\ast}
.

Assumption 10
.

(i)
(GM)^{2/p}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G},m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1/2}=o(1)
; (ii)
(GM)(nT)^{-1/2}=O(1)
.

Assumption
10
(i) is used to establish the consistency of the
M-estimator. It accommodates a wide range of grouping structures, including
Examples 1–4 in Section
G
of the Online Appendix as
special cases. To better illustrate the restriction imposed by Assumption
10
(i) on the number of groups, let’s consider a simple scenario where
the sizes of the groups
I_{g}
(
g\in\mathcal{G}
) are proportional to
each other and the sizes of
I_{m}
(
m\in\mathcal{G}
) are also
proportional to each other, i.e.,

K^{-1}\leq\min\left\{\frac{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}}{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}},\frac{\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}}{\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}}\right\}\leq\max\left\{\frac{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}},\frac{\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}}{\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}}\right\}\leq K,

where
K
denotes a generic finite constant. In this case

K^{-1}\leq n(Gn_{g})^{-1}\leq K\text{ \ \ and \ \ }K^{-1}\leq T(MT_{m})^{-1}\leq K

(90)

for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
and any
m\in\mathcal{M}
. Under (
90
), Assumption
10
(i) becomes

(GM)^{1/2+2/p}(nT)^{-1/2}=o(1)

(91)

which requires that the number of groups
GM
is of a smaller order of
nT
.
Assumption
10
(ii) is used to establish the
(nT)^{-1/2}
-consistency
of
\hat{\theta}
.

Let
\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{\gamma}(z_{i,t})
, where
\psi_{\gamma}(z_{i,t})\equiv\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
. Define
\hat{U}_{\gamma,g,m}\equiv\hat{\Psi}_{\theta\gamma,g,m}-\Psi_{\theta\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}
, and for
j=1,\ldots,d_{\gamma}
,

\Psi_{\gamma_{j}\gamma\gamma,g,m}\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\gamma\partial\gamma^{\top}}\left(\frac{\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\right)\right].

The matrix
\Psi_{\theta_{j}\gamma\gamma,g,m}
is defined in the similar
way for
j=1,\ldots,d_{\theta}
. Theorem
A1
below provides
the second order expansion of the estimation error in
\hat{\theta}
.

Theorem A1
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9

and
10
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\ast}

\displaystyle=H_{\theta\theta}^{-1}(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})(\hat{\Psi}_{\theta,g,m}-\Psi_{\theta\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m})

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ }+H_{\theta\theta}^{-1}(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})B_{\theta,g,m}+o_{p}\left((nT)^{-1/2}\right),

where

\displaystyle H_{\theta\theta}

\displaystyle\equiv-(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})(\Psi_{\theta\theta,g,m}-\Psi_{\theta\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\Psi_{\gamma\theta,g,m}),

\displaystyle B_{\theta,g,m}

\displaystyle\equiv 2^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\Psi_{\theta_{j}\gamma\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}\right)_{j\leq d_{\theta}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{U}_{\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}\right]

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ }-2^{-1}\Psi_{\theta\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\Psi_{\gamma_{j}\gamma\gamma,g,m}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}\right)_{j\leq d_{\gamma}}\right].

Theorem
A2
establishes a second-order expansion of the likelihood
function evaluated at the M-estimator
\hat{\phi}\equiv(\hat{\theta}^{\top},\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},1}^{\top},\ldots,\hat{\gamma}_{\mathcal{G},M}^{\top})^{\top}
.

Theorem A2
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9
and
10
, we have

L_{n,T}(\hat{\phi})=L_{n,T}(\phi^{\ast})+2^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}^{\top}(-\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m})^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}+o_{p}\left((GM)^{1/2}\right),

where
L_{n,T}(\hat{\phi})=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi(z_{i,t};\hat{\theta},\hat{\gamma}_{g,m})
, and
L_{n,T}(\phi^{\ast})
is defined analogously with
\hat{\phi}
replaced by the pseudo true parameter
\phi^{\ast}
.

Let
\hat{\Psi}_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m})\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi(z_{i,t};\theta,\gamma_{g,m})
. Then the joint
likelihood evaluated at the pseudo true parameter can be written as
L_{n,T}(\phi^{\ast})=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})
. Define

R_{n,T}^{\ast}\equiv\frac{(nT)^{-1/2}}{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}^{\top}(-\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1})\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}].

With this notation, Theorem
A2
implies the following expansion for
the maximized joint likelihood:

\displaystyle(nT)^{-1/2}L_{n,T}(\hat{\phi})-R_{n,T}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=(nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})]

(92)

\displaystyle\text{ \ }+(nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})(\hat{\Psi}_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{g,m}(\phi_{g,m}^{\ast})])

(93)

\displaystyle\text{ \ }-(4nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}(n_{g}T_{m})(\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,g,m}])

(94)

\displaystyle\text{ \ }+o_{p}\left((GM)^{1/2}(nT)^{-1/2}\right),

(95)

where the first term (
92
) is the population likelihood function
evaluated at the pseudo true parameter, the second term (
93
) and
the third term (
94
) are the first-order term and the second-order
term respectively, (
95
) represents the higher order term dominated
by the term (
94
). The second term (
93
) plus the third term
(
94
) after self-normalization/studentization can be approximated
by a standard normal random variable, which as we shall see in the next
section, enables us to achieve valid model comparison inference uniformly
over different model structures.

C.3
Generic QLR: Expansion and Limit Theory

Suppose that, for the same data set
\left\{z_{i,t}\right\}_{i\leq n,t\leq T}
, we consider two models
j=1,2
. For model
j
, the joint log-likelihood
function is

\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{i\leq n}\log f_{j}(z_{i,t};\theta_{j},\gamma_{j,i,t}),

where
f_{j}\left(\cdot\right)
is known,
\theta_{j}
collects
individual- and time-invariant parameters, and
\gamma_{j,i,t}\equiv\gamma_{g_{j}(i),m_{j}(t)}
denotes fixed effects indexed by known group
assignment function
g_{j}(\cdot)
and
m_{j}(\cdot)
with ranges
\mathcal{G}_{j}=\{1,\ldots,G_{j}\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{j}=\{1,\ldots,M_{j}\}
respectively. For any
i
and
t
with
g_{j}(i)=g
and
m_{j}(t)=m
, let

\psi_{j}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{j},\gamma_{j,g,m}\right)\equiv\log f_{j}(z_{i,t};\theta_{j},\gamma_{j,i,t}).

Then the joint log-likelihood function can be written as

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{j}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{j},\gamma_{j,g,m}\right)\equiv\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{j,g,m}(\phi_{j,g,m}),

where
\hat{\Psi}_{j,g,m}(\phi_{j,g,m})\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{j}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{j},\gamma_{j,g,m}\right)
and
\phi_{j,g,m}\equiv(\theta_{j}^{\top},\gamma_{j,g,m}^{\top})^{\top}
. The two models may differ in their
specifications of the likelihood functions
f_{j}(\cdot)
and/or the group
assignment functions
g_{j}(\cdot)
and/or
m_{j}(\cdot)
.

Let
\phi_{j,i,t}^{\ast}\equiv(\theta_{j}^{\ast\top},\gamma_{j,i,t}^{\ast\top})^{\top}
denote the pseudo true parameters of model
j
. The Vuong test compares two models by testing

H_{0}:\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t})\right]=0,\text{ where }\Delta\psi(z_{i,t})\equiv\psi_{1}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i,t}^{\ast})-\psi_{2}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,i,t}^{\ast})

(96)

using the QLR statistic

QLR_{n,T}\equiv(nT)^{-1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{1,g,m}(\hat{\phi}_{1,g,m})-\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{2,g,m}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g,m})\right),

(97)

where
\hat{\phi}_{j,g,m}\equiv(\hat{\theta}_{j}^{\top},\hat{\gamma}_{j,g,m}^{\top})^{\top}\
denotes the estimator of
\phi_{j,g,m}^{\ast}

in model
j
.

Let
\tilde{\Psi}_{j,i}\equiv T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i,t}^{\ast})
and
\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}\equiv(n_{g}T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g,m}^{\ast})
. To study the properties of the QLR
statistic, we suppose that Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9

and
10
hold for model 1 and model 2, which enables us to apply the
expansion of the likelihood function listed in (
92
)-(
95
)
to both models to obtain

QLR_{n,T}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i})+S_{n,T}+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

(98)

where

S_{n,T}\equiv\sum_{j=1,2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\frac{(-1)^{j}(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}}{2(nT)^{1/2}}.

Since
z_{i,t}
are independent across
i
for all
t
,
\hat{\Psi}_{1,i}-\hat{\Psi}_{2,i}
are independent across
i
. Consequently, the asymptotic
distribution of
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i})
is straightforward to derive. However, when the two models are
nested or overlapping non-nested, its variance may be zero or arbitrarily
close to zero, invalidating inference based solely on this leading term. To
conduct valid inference for (
92
) in a more general setting, the
second term
S_{n,T}
on the right-hand side of (
98
) must therefore
be taken into account when deriving the asymptotic distribution of the QLR
statistic.

For this purpose, we define

\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}\equiv T_{m}^{-1/2}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\tilde{\psi}_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g,m}^{\ast})\text{,}

where
\tilde{\psi}_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g,m}^{\ast})\equiv(-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g,m})^{-1/2}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g,m}^{\ast})
for any
i\in I_{g}
and
t\in I_{m}
, and for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}

and
m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
. Then for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}
, we obtain
24
24

24

We adopt the convention that the summaion over an empty set is zero.
Therefore under this trandition, the summation
\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,i^{\prime}m}=0
if the set
\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g}:

i^{\prime}<i\}
is empty.

\frac{(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}}{2T^{1/2}}=\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}}{2n_{g}T^{1/2}}+\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i^{\prime},m}}{n_{g}T^{1/2}}.

(99)

The decomposition in (
99
) is useful to study
QLR_{n,T}
with
S_{n,T}
taken into account, since by the independence of
z_{i,t}
across
i
for all
t
,
\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}
are independent across
i
for all
m
, and
\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i^{\prime},m}
are uncorrelated across
i
for all
m
.

To employ the decomposition in (
99
) in derivation of the
asymptotic distribution of
QLR_{n,T}
, we define

\tilde{V}_{j,i}\equiv\frac{\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}}{2n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2}}\text{ \ \ \ and \ \ \ }\tilde{U}_{j,i}\equiv\frac{\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i^{\prime},m}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2}}

(100)

for
j=1,2
and for any
i=1,\ldots,n
. Then

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\frac{(n_{g}T_{m})\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}^{\top}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g,m}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g,m}}{2T^{1/2}}=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}(\tilde{V}_{j,i}+\tilde{U}_{j,i})=\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{V}_{j,i}+\tilde{U}_{j,i}),

and the expansion of the QLR statistic in (
98
) can be written as

QLR_{n,T}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}+\tilde{U}_{i})+o_{p}(n^{-1/2})

(101)

where

\tilde{\Psi}_{i}\equiv\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i},\quad\tilde{V}_{i}\equiv\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i},\quad\tilde{U}_{i}\equiv\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i}.

(102)

Let
\mathcal{F}_{nT,i}
denote the sigma field generated by
\left\{z_{i^{\prime}t}\right\}_{t\leq T,i^{\prime}\leq i}
. Then by the
independence of
\left\{z_{i,t}\right\}_{t\leq T}
across
i
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{U}_{i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]=0

(103)

for any
i=1,\ldots,n
, where
\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}\equiv\tilde{\Psi}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{i}]
and
\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast}\equiv\tilde{V}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{V}_{i}]
. This shows that
\{\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{U}_{i}\}_{i\leq n}
forms a martingale
difference array. In view of the expansion of the QLR statistic in (
101
) and the martingale property in (
103
), an inference
procedure which is valid uniformly over the model relationship can be
established by the martingale CLT.

Let
\omega_{n,T}\equiv(n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i}+\tilde{U}_{i}))^{1/2}
. The following assumption is imposed to
derive the asymptotic distribution of
QLR_{n,T}
.

Assumption 11
.

(i)
n\omega_{n,T}^{2}\geq K^{-1}
; (ii)
(n\omega_{n,T}^{2})^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{U}_{i})\rightarrow_{d}N(0,1)
as
n,T\rightarrow\infty
.

Assumption
11
(i) ensures that the second term in the expansion of the
QLR statistic later in (
101
) is asymptotically negligible, while
Assumption
11
(ii) requires that the leading term in the expansion
given in (
11
) admits an asymptotic normal distribution.
Assumption
11
(ii) can be derived by verifying the sufficient
conditions of the martingale CLT (e.g., Corollary 3.1 of
26
). Specifically, let

\xi_{nT,i}\equiv(n\omega_{n,T}^{2})^{-1/2}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{U}_{i}).

(104)

Then Assumption
11
(ii) holds under the following conditions:

\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{nT,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]\rightarrow_{p}1,

(105)

and for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{nT,i}^{2}I\{|\xi_{nT,i}|>\varepsilon\}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]\rightarrow_{p}0.

(106)

Details on verification of Assumption
11
(ii) under
d_{\gamma}=1
and
some low level conditions are provided in Theorem
A4
below.

Theorem A3
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9
and
10
hold for model 1 and model 2. Then under
Assumption
11
, we have

\frac{QLR_{n,T}-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]-\overline{QLR}_{n,T}}{\omega_{n,T}}\rightarrow_{d}\text{ }N(0,\text{ }1)

(107)

where
\overline{QLR}_{n,T}\equiv n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{i}]
.

Proof of Theorem
A3
. Since Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9
and
10
hold for both models, we can
invoke (
101
) and then apply Assumption
11
(i) to get

\frac{QLR_{n,T}-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]-QLR_{n,T}^{\ast}}{\omega_{n,T}}=(n\omega_{n,T}^{2})^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{U}_{i})+o_{p}\left(1\right)

which together with Assumption
11
(ii) and Slutsky’s theorem shows (
107
).
Q.E.D.

We conclude this section by presenting low-level sufficient conditions for
verifying Assumption
11
(ii) in the case
d_{\gamma}=1
. Let
A_{j,i}\equiv(\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m})_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}
for
i=1,\ldots,n
. Then

\tilde{V}_{j,i}=\frac{A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i}}{2n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2}}\text{ \ \ \ \ \ and \ \ \ \ }\tilde{U}_{j,i}=\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}\frac{A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i^{\prime}}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2}}.

Assumption 12
.

(i)
\mathbb{E}\left[(\Delta\psi(z_{i,t}))^{4}\omega_{n,T}^{-4}\right]<K
; (ii)
\mathbb{E}\left[|\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i,t}^{\ast})|^{4+\delta}\right]<K
; (iii)
G_{j}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{g}^{-1}=o(1)
.

Assumptions
12
(i, ii) are used to show the Lindeberg condition for the
martingale CLT and to bound the moments of
\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,i,m}
.
Assumption
12
(iii) requires that under both model 1 and model 2, the
number of the groups and/or the size of the smallest group across
i
should
go to infinity.

Theorem A4
.

Assumption
11
(ii) holds under Assumptions
1
,
10
(ii),
11
(i) and
12
.

The proof of this theorem is provided in the Online Appendix.

Appendix D
Proofs for Lemmas in Appendix
A.1

Proof of Lemma
1
. (a) First, by Assumptions
1
and
8
(iii), we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i}^{\ast})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KT^{-1}\sum_{0\leq h\leq T}\sum_{t\leq T-h}\left|\mathrm{Cov}(\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i}^{\ast}),\psi_{j}(z_{i,t+h};\phi_{j,i}^{\ast}))\right|

(S1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KT^{-1}\sum_{0\leq h\leq T}\sum_{t\leq T-h}\max_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i}^{\ast})||_{2+\delta}^{2}\alpha_{i}^{\delta/(2+\delta)}(h)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i}^{\ast})||_{2+\delta}^{2}\sum_{0\leq h\leq T}(1-h/T)a^{\delta h/(2+\delta)}\leq K,

where the second inequality follows from the covariance inequality for
strong mixing processes (see, e.g., Corollary 14.3 in
13
),
and the last inequality uses Assumption
1
(iv). Therefore,

T^{1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{V}_{j,i}]=2\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i}^{\ast})\right)^{2}\right]\leq KG_{j}.

It follows that

\left|T^{1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{V}_{i}]\right|=\left|T^{1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{V}_{1,i}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{V}_{2,i}])\right|\leq K(G_{1}+G_{2}).

(S2)

Moreover, by Assumptions
8
(iii) and
9
, and (
55
) (which follows from Lemma
2
under Assumptions
1
and
2
without imposing Assumption
3
), we obtain

R_{j,n,T}(\hat{\phi}_{j})=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sigma_{j,\gamma,i}^{2}+O_{p}(1)=O_{p}(G_{j}).

(S3)

Combining (
S2
) and (
S3
) with Theorem
1
, we
obtain

\displaystyle MQLR_{n,T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{QLR}_{n,T}+(MQLR_{n,T}^{\ast}-\overline{QLR}_{n,T})

(S4)

\displaystyle-(R_{1,n,T}(\hat{\phi}_{j})-R_{2,n,T}(\hat{\phi}_{j}))+T^{1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{V}_{i}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{QLR}_{n,T}+O_{p}(\omega_{n,T}+G_{1}+G_{2})=\overline{QLR}_{n,T}+O_{p}(G_{1}+G_{2}),

where the last equality follows from Lemma
5
(ii). From the
definition of
\hat{\omega}_{n,T}^{2}
and Lemma
S20
,

\hat{\omega}_{n,T}^{2}\leq\max\left\{\hat{\sigma}_{n,T}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{U,n,T}^{2},\hat{\sigma}_{U,n,T}^{2}\right\}\leq K,

(S5)

with probability approaching 1. It follows from (
S4
) and (
S5
) that

\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{n,T}^{2\text{-side}}(p)]=\mathbb{P}\left(|MQLR_{n,T}|\geq\hat{\omega}_{n,T}z_{1-p/2}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(|\overline{QLR}_{n,T}|\geq Kz_{1-p/2}+O_{p}(G_{1}+G_{2})\right)-o(1).

Since
|\overline{QLR}_{n,T}|\succ G_{1}+G_{2}
, the right-hand side
converges to 1. This establishes
\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{n,T}^{2\text{-side}}(p)]\rightarrow 1
, as
n,T\rightarrow\infty
.

(b) The proof for the one-sided test follows by analogous arguments and is
therefore omitted.
Q.E.D.

)

.

Proof of lemma
3
.
To prove the claim in (
77
), we first note that, by Lemma
S12
,

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t},\hat{\phi}_{i})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{T}[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t})^{2}])

(S6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(nT)^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\Delta\psi(z_{i,t})\Delta\psi_{\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})(\hat{\phi}_{i}-\phi_{i}^{\ast})

\displaystyle+(nT)^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\phi}_{i}-\phi_{i}^{\ast})^{\top}\Delta\psi_{\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})^{\top}\Delta\psi_{\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})(\hat{\phi}_{i}-\phi_{i}^{\ast})

\displaystyle+O_{p}(T^{-3/2}+\omega_{n,T}T^{-1}+\omega_{n,T}^{2}(nT)^{-1/2}).

Together with Lemma
S8
, Lemma
S17
and
Assumption
1
(i), this implies

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t},\hat{\phi}_{i})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{T}[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t})^{2}])

(S7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(nT)^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\phi}_{i}-\phi_{i}^{\ast})^{\top}\Delta\psi_{\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})^{\top}\Delta\psi_{\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})(\hat{\phi}_{i}-\phi_{i}^{\ast})

\displaystyle+O_{p}(T^{-3/2}+\omega_{n,T}(nT)^{-1/2}).

Under Assumption
3
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{\psi}_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}\left(z_{i,t}\right)\right)^{2}\right]=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\psi}_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}\left(z_{i,t}\right)^{2}]=\sigma_{j,\gamma,i}^{2}

and

T^{-1}\sum_{t,t^{\prime}\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\psi}_{1,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\tilde{\psi}_{2,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t^{\prime}})]=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\psi}_{1,\gamma}^{\ast}\left(z_{i,t}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{1,\gamma}^{\ast}\left(z_{i,t}\right)]=\sigma_{12,\gamma,i},

where
\tilde{\psi}_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})/(-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{1/2}
(
j=1,2
), for any
i\in I_{2,g}
and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}
. Combining these results with
Assumption
1
(i), (
S7
) and Lemma
S11

yields

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t},\hat{\phi}_{i})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{T}[\Delta\psi(z_{i,t})^{2}])

(S8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sigma_{1,\gamma,i}^{4}+n_{2,g}^{-2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}s_{j,\gamma,i}^{2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sigma_{j,\gamma,i}^{2}-2n_{2,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sigma_{12,\gamma,i}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+O_{p}(T^{-3/2}+\omega_{n,T}(nT)^{-1/2}).

Combining (
S8
) with the definition of
\sigma_{S,n,T}^{2}

establishes the claim in (
77
). The result in (
78
) is
proved in Lemma
S16
.

D.1
Auxiliary Lemmas

This section provides the lemmas used in the proof of Lemmas
2
-
4
. We begin by recalling notation introduced in the main text and then
introduce additional notation. For
j=1,2
, and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle n_{j,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}\left[\hat{\psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}\left(z_{i,t}\right)\right]\text{,}

\displaystyle\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle n_{j,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\mathbb{E}_{T}\left[\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}\left(z_{i,t}\right)\right]\text{, }

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle n_{j,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\psi_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})],

where
\hat{\psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}\left(z_{i,t}\right)\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\hat{\phi}_{j,g})
,
\psi_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}\left[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right]
,
\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}\left(z_{i,t}\right)\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})
and
\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})
.

Lemma S1
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2

hold. For model
j
(
j=1,2
), we have:

(i)
\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}|\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}|=o_{p}(1)
;

(ii)
\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(-\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}))\geq K^{-1}
wpa1;

(iii)
\hat{\theta}_{j}=\theta_{j}^{\ast}+O_{p}((nT)^{-1/2})
;

(iv)
\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}|(n_{j,g}T)^{1/2}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast})|^{s}=O_{p}\left(G_{j}\right)
for
s\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
;

(v)
\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1});

(vi)
\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}|\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}+\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}|=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2})
;

(vii)
\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}+\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g})^{2}=O_{p}(T^{-1})
;

(viii)
\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}^{4}]=O_{p}(T^{-2})
.

Proof of Lemma
S1
. We begin the proof
by noting that Assumptions
7
,
8
,
9
hold for both models
1 and 2 under Assumption
2
(i). Additionally, Assumption
10

has been verified for both models under Assumption
1
(i) in the proof
of Theorem
1
. Under these assumptions, the claims in parts (i),
(ii), (iii), and (iv) of the lemma are established using Lemma
S58
, Lemma
S60
(ii), Lemma
S62
, and Lemma
S65
from Section
C
, respectively.

To establish the claim in part (v) of the lemma, we begin by applying the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}\leq 2\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}+2\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2},

(S9)

where
\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}\equiv\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})
and
\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})
is defined analogously to
\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})
with
\hat{\phi}_{j,g}
replaced by
\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}
. Under
Assumptions
1
and
8
(iii), we obtain:

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}\right]=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\right|^{2}\right]\leq KG_{j}T^{-1}.

By Markov’s inequality, it follows that:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}=O_{p}\left(G_{j}T^{-1}\right).

(S10)

Next, using Assumption
8
(i) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we
have:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}))^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{2}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{4}\right)^{1/2}\times\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{4}\right)^{1/2}.

(S11)

The second term in the product satisfies:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{4}=O_{p}(G_{j})

(S12)

by Assumption
8
(ii) and Markov’s inequality. From parts (i) and (iii)
of the lemma, we know:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{2}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{4}=K\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}^{2}|\hat{\gamma}_{g,m}-\gamma_{g,m}^{\ast}|^{4}+n^{2}||\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast}||^{4}\right)=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-2}),

(S13)

which, together with (
S11
)-(
S13
),
implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}=O_{p}\left(G_{j}T^{-1}\right).

(S14)

The claim in part (v) of the lemma follows from (
S9
), (
S10
) and (
S14
).

To establish the claim in part (vi) of the lemma, observe that from (
S635
) of Lemma
S61
:

(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast})+\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}=-\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast})-\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{2\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}},

(S15)

where

\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}\equiv(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{\partial\gamma_{j,g}\partial\theta_{j}^{\top}}\text{ and }\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}\equiv(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})}{\partial\phi_{j,g}\partial\phi_{j,g}^{\top}}.

Here,
\tilde{\phi}_{j,g}
lies between
\hat{\phi}_{j,g}
and
\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}
. By Assumption
8
(ii), the triangle inequality, and
Markov’s inequality, we have

n^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}||\leq(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{\partial\gamma_{j,g}\partial\theta_{j}^{\top}}\right\|=O_{p}(1).

(S16)

This, combined with parts (ii) and (iii) of the lemma, shows:

\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast})\right|\leq\frac{||\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast}||\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}||}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}|}=O_{P}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}).

(S17)

By the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right|\leq\frac{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}||}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}|}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}.

(S18)

In (
S640
) of the Supplementary Appendix, we have shown that
under Assumptions
1
and
7
-
10
:

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}||=O_{p}(1).

(S19)

Using parts (iii, iv) of the lemma, we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}\leq K\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}|\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}|^{2}+n||\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast}||^{2}\right)=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(S20)

From part (ii) of the lemma and (
S18
)-(
S20
), it follows that:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(S21)

Combining this result with (
S15
) and (
S17
) establishes the claim in this part.

To establish the claim in part (vii), similar arguments as in (
S16
) yield:

n^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}||^{2}\leq(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left\|\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{\partial\gamma_{j,g}\partial\theta_{j}^{\top}}\right\|^{2}=O_{p}(1).

Combining this with parts (ii) and (iii) of the lemma, we obtain:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}\left|\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast})\right|^{2}\leq\frac{||\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast}||^{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\theta,g}||^{2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{2}}=O_{P}(T^{-1}).

(S22)

The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, part (ii) of the lemma, and earlier bounds in
(
S13
) and (
S19
) further imply:

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}\left|\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left\|\tilde{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\phi\phi,g}\right\|^{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{j,g}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{4}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{2}}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-2}).

(S23)

The claim in part (vii) of the lemma now follows from (
S15
), (
S22
) and (
S23
).

To establish the claim in part (viii), observe that:

n_{j,g}^{2}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}^{4}=T^{-2}\left((n_{j,g}T)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right)^{4}.

(S24)

Using Rosenthal’s inequality (see, e.g., (
S565
) in Lemma
S47
) and
Assumptions
2
(iii), we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left((n_{j,g}T)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right)^{4}\right]\leq K.

(S25)

The claim follows from (
S24
) and (
S25
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma S2
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
,
we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\hat{\psi}_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{j,g}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}=O_{p}\left((nT^{-1})^{1/2}+G_{j}T^{-1}\right).

(S26)

Proof of Lemma
S2
. Define:

\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g})\equiv\frac{\partial\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g})}{\partial\phi_{j,g}}\text{ \ \ and \ \ }\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g})\equiv\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g})}{\partial\phi_{j,g}\partial\phi_{j,g}^{\top}}.

(S27)

Using the Taylor expansion, we obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t}))

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})

\displaystyle+(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})

\displaystyle+(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}),

(S28)

where
\tilde{\phi}_{j,g}
lies between
\hat{\phi}_{j,g}
and
\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}
for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}
. From this expression, it is
evident that (
S26
) follows if the following conditions
are satisfied:

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\frac{\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}+G_{j}T^{-1}),

(S29)

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}),

(S30)

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

(S31)

We now proceed to verify (
S29
), (
S30
) and (
S31
).

Let
C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t},\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})
and
C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\equiv C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})]
.
Applying Assumptions
1
,
8
, and the covariance inequality for
strong mixing processes, we obtain:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq K.

(S32)

Using the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it follows
that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left|(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right|}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left\|(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|)}.

(S33)

Under Assumption
1
and (
S32
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left\|(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}KT\leq KG_{j}T^{-1},

which, combined with Markov’s inequality, implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left\|(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right\|^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

(S34)

Thus, by collecting the results in Lemma
S1
(ii), (
S20
), (
S33
) and (
S34
), we conclude:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{1,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}\right)(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})=O_{p}\left(G_{j}T^{-1}\right).

(S35)

By the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})](\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{T\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}|)}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left\|(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})]\right\|^{2}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(S36)

Since
\left\|\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})]\right\|\leq K
under
Assumption
8
(iii), by (
S20
) and the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left\|(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{1t,j,g}(z_{i,t})]\right\|^{2}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

(S37)

Combining this with Assumption
9
, which is upheld in Assumption
2
(i), Lemma
S1
(ii, v) and (
S36
),
it follows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})](\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

(S38)

By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\theta}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast})\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|,

(S39)

where
\psi_{j,\gamma a}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g})\equiv\partial\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g})/\partial a
for
a\in\{\theta_{j},\gamma_{j,g}\}
.
The first term in the inequality above can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\theta}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left\|\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\theta}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]\right\|}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})}||\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast}||

\displaystyle\leq KG_{j}\left\|\hat{\theta}_{j}-\theta_{j}^{\ast}\right\|=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}).

(S40)

where the first inequality follows from the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz
inequalities, the second inequality follows from Assumptions
8
(iii)
and
9
, and the equality holds due to Lemma
S1
(iii).
For the second term after the inequality in (
S39
), we
have:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}+\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g})\right|.

(S41)

Using Assumptions
8
(iii) and
9
, and Lemma
S1
(vi), we get

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}+\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g})\right|

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}|\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast}+\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}|=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}).

(S42)

By Assumptions
1
,
8
and
9
, and Lemma
S1
(viii), it follows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}\right|^{2}\right]\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma,g}^{2}]\leq KnT^{-1}.

This, together with Markov’s inequality, (
S39
)-(
S42
) shows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{1,j,g}(z_{i,t})]}{(n_{j,g}T)\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}).

(S43)

Collecting the results in (
S35
), (
S38
) and (
S43
), we obtain

T^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\frac{\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{n_{j,g}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}=O_{p}\left((nT^{-1})^{1/2}+G_{j}T^{-1}\right),

which establishes (
S29
).

We now proceed to verify (
S30
). Using the triangle
inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})||^{2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|)}.

(S44)

By Assumption
8
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have:

\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})||^{2}\leq K\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(M(z_{i,t})^{2}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}+||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})||^{2}\right).

This implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})||^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{4}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{2}

\displaystyle+K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})||^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(1+\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}\right)\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})^{2},

(S45)

where
\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})\equiv M(z_{i,t})+||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})||
. By the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{4}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})^{4}\right)^{1/2}.

(S46)

From Assumptions
8
(ii, iii) and Markov’s inequality, it follows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})^{4}]\leq O_{p}(G_{j}),

(S47)

which together with (
S13
) and (
S46
) establishes that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

This along with Lemma
S1
(i, ii) and (
S45
) shows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})||^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

(S48)

Combining Lemma
S1
(ii), and results (
S44
) and (
S48
), we verify that:

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}^{\top}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}),

which shows that (
S30
) holds.

To verify (
S31
), we first note that, by the triangle
inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\right\|\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\right\|}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|)}.

(S49)

Using Assumption
8
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\right\|\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{2}+\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ }+||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})(\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|+\left\|\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})\right\|)

\displaystyle\leq K(1+\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}+||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||))\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{2}(z_{i,t})^{2},

where
\tilde{M}_{2}(z_{i,t})\equiv\tilde{M}_{1}(z_{i,t})+||\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})||
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})\psi_{j,\gamma\phi\phi}(z_{i,t};\tilde{\phi}_{j,g})(\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast})}{(n_{j,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(1+\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}+||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||))}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|)}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ }\times\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{2}(z_{i,t})^{2}.

(S50)

Following similar arguments to those used to establish (
S48
), we can show that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}||\hat{\phi}_{j,g}-\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{M}_{2}(z_{i,t})^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}),

which, combined with (
S50
) and Lemma
S1
(i, ii, iii), verifies (
S31
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma S3
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
,
we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])^{2}-(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])^{2}}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}+G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S51)

Proof of Lemma
S3
. First, we notice
that the left-hand side (LHS) of (
S51
) can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{(\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}))^{2}-(\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}))^{2}}{n_{2,g}T^{2}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{(\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})))^{2}}{n_{2,g}T^{2}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}))}{n_{2,g}T^{2}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}).

(S52)

By Assumption
8
(i) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the first term
on the right-hand side (RHS) of the above equation can be bounded as:

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{(\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})))^{2}}{n_{2,g}T^{2}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{2,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})||\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}||)^{2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}||\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}||^{4}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{4}\right)^{1/2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}.

(S53)

From Assumption
8
and Markov’s inequality, it follows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{4}=O_{p}(G_{2}),

(S54)

which along with (
S13
) and (
S53
)
implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{(\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})))^{2}}{n_{2,g}T^{2}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S55)

To investigate the second term in the RHS of (
S52
), we
first apply the Taylor expansion to obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}))}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t},\tilde{\phi}_{2,g})(\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}),

(S56)

where
\tilde{\phi}_{j,g}
lies between
\hat{\phi}_{j,g}
and
\phi_{j,g}^{\ast}
. By Assumption
8
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t},\tilde{\phi}_{2,g})-\psi_{2,\gamma\phi}(z_{i,t},\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}))(\tilde{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}||\tilde{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}.

(S57)

Note that by a maximal inequality under the
L_{p}
-norm,

\left\|\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right|\right\|_{p}\leq Kn^{1/p}\max_{i\leq n}\left\|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right\|_{p}.

(S58)

By Assumption
8
, we have

\mathbb{E}[|\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})|^{p}]\leq K,

(S59)

which implies that
\left\|\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\right\|_{p}\leq K
. Thus, applying Rosenthal’s
inequality, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right|^{p}\right]\leq KT^{p/2}

which along with (
S58
) shows that

\left\|\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right|\right\|_{p}\leq Kn^{1/p}T^{-1/2}.

Combining this result with Markov’s inequality and Assumption
1
(i),
we establish that

\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right|=O_{p}(T^{-1/2+1/p}).

(S60)

Therefore, from (
S59
) and (
S60
),
it follows that

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right|

\displaystyle+\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\right|\overset{}{=}O_{p}(1).

(S61)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Assumption
8
(ii) and (
S13
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}||\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}||^{2}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t}).}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}||\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}||^{4}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}M(z_{i,t})^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S62)

From (
S56
), (
S57
), (
S61
) and (
S62
), we establish:

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}))}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma\theta}(z_{i,t})(\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})

\displaystyle+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})+O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S63)

The first term on the RHS of (
S63
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma\theta}(z_{i,t})(\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{||\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast}||\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{2,\gamma\theta}(z_{i,t})||.

(S64)

By Assumption
8
and Markov’s inequality, it follows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(n_{2,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}||\psi_{2,\gamma\theta}(z_{i,t})||=O_{p}(G_{2}),

which together with Lemma
S1
(ii, iii) and (
S61
) implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma\theta}(z_{i,t})(\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})=O_{p}(G_{2}(nT)^{-1/2}).

(S65)

To study the second term on the RHS of (
S63
), we
first write it as

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})])}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})

\displaystyle+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])

\displaystyle-\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g})

\displaystyle+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}.

(S66)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can bound the first term on the RHS of (
S66
) as

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})])}{(n_{2,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max_{i\leq n}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{j,\gamma}(z_{i,t})\right|}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{j,g}-\gamma_{j,g}^{\ast})^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{2,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(S67)

Under Assumptions
1
and
8
, we can apply the covariance
inequality for mixing processes to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{2,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right)^{2}\right]\leq KG_{2}T^{-1},

which together with Markov’s inequality, Lemma
S1
(ii, iv),
(
S61
) and (
S67
) implies that
we can bound the first term on the RHS of (
S66
) by

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})])}{(n_{2,g}T)\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S68)

As for the second term on the RHS of (
S66
), we have
by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Assumption
8

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{2,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(S69)

Under Assumptions
3
and
8
, it follows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{2,g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])\right)^{2}\right]\leq KG_{2}T^{-1},

which together with Markov’s inequality, Lemma
S1
(ii, iv)
and (
S69
) implies that we can bound the second term
on the RHS of (
S66
) by

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})])=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S70)

As for the third term on the RHS of (
S66
), we have by
the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g})|}=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1})

(S71)

where the equality follows from Assumption
9
and Lemma
S1
(iv, v).

Combining the results from (
S63
), (
S65
), (
S66
), (
S68
), (
S70
) and (
S71
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\psi}_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})-\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t}))}{n_{2,g}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}+O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S72)

In view of the results established in (
S52
), (
S55
) and (
S72
), it is evident that
the claim of the lemma follows if

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}=O_{p}(G_{2}^{1/2}T^{-1/2}).

(S73)

We next turn to verify (
S73
). Using the expression in
(
S15
), we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\gamma}_{2,g}-\gamma_{2,g}^{\ast})\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}}{\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1})\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}(\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast})}{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\phi\phi,g}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast})}{2\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}.

(S74)

By Assumptions
1
,
2
(iii) and
8
, and Lemma
S1
(viii)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}}{\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}^{2}]\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{2}}\right)^{2}\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}^{2}]\leq KG_{2}T^{-1},

where the second inequality follows from (
S25
).

Thus, by Markov’s inequality,

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}}{\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}=O_{p}(G_{2}^{1/2}T^{-1/2}).

(S75)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Assumptions
8
and
9
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1})\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|},

which along with Lemma
S1
(ii, v), (
S25
) and Markov’s inequality shows that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1}-\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}^{-1})\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma,g}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S76)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Assumption
9
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}(\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast})}{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K||\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast}||}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g})|}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}(||\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}]||+||\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}]||).

(S77)

Under Assumptions
1
and
8
, we can apply the covariance
inequality for mixing processes to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[||\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}]||^{2}\right]\leq K(n_{2,g}T)^{-1},

which together with Markov’s inequality, Assumption
8
, Lemma
S1
(ii, iii) and (
S77
) implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\theta,g}(\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{\ast})}{\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}=O_{p}(G_{2}T^{-1}).

(S78)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Assumptions
8
and
9
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\frac{(\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast})^{\top}\tilde{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\phi\phi,g}(\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast})}{2\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2,g}}\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t})]\mathbb{E}[\psi_{2,\gamma}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{2,g}\Psi_{2,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}||\tilde{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\phi\phi,g}||}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}|\hat{\Psi}_{2,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})|}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}||\hat{\phi}_{2,g}-\phi_{2,g}^{\ast}||^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1})

(S79)

where the equality follows from Lemma
S1
(ii), (
S19
) and (
S20
). The claim in (
S73
) follows directly from (
S74
), (
S75
), (
S76
), (
S78
) and (
S79
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma S4
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
,
we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{j,g}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}-\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{T}[\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})]}{n_{j,g}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)=O_{p}((nT^{-1})^{1/2}+G_{j}T^{-1}).

Proof of Lemma
S4
. Let
C_{2,j,g}(z_{i,t})\equiv\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})
and
C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\equiv C_{2,j,g}(z_{i,t})-\mathbb{E}[C_{2,j,g}(z_{i,t})]
. Using these notations, the following expression is
obtained:

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(\frac{\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})}{n_{j,g}\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}-\frac{\psi_{j,\gamma}^{2}(z_{i,t})}{n_{j,g}\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}-\frac{1}{\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[C_{2,j,g}(z_{i,t})]

\displaystyle+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}-\frac{1}{\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t}).

(S80)

Applying the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can
bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
S80
)
as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}-\frac{1}{\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left|(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right|}{\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})-\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}((n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t}))^{2}\right)^{1/2}}{T\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(|\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}|)}.

(S81)

Using similar arguments to those leading up to (
S34
),
we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left((n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})\right)^{2}=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}),

which together with Assumption
9
, Lemma
S1
(ii, v)
and (
S81
) implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\Psi}_{j,\gamma\gamma}(\hat{\phi}_{j,g})}-\frac{1}{\Psi_{j,\gamma\gamma,g}}\right)(n_{j,g}T)^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{j,g}}\sum_{t\leq T}C_{2,j,g}^{\ast}(z_{i,t})=O_{p}(G_{j}T^{-1}).

(S82)

Next, observe that the first term on the right-hand side of (
S80
) can be rewritten as
