Lemma S69
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
A4
,
we have

(n\omega_{n,T}^{2})^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i})\tilde{U}_{j,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]=O_{p}\left(T^{-1/2}+G_{j}^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{g}^{-1}\right).

(S725)

Proof of Lemma
S69
. For any
i=1,\ldots,n
, by the independence of
\{z_{i,t}\}_{t\leq T}
across
i
we have

\frac{n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i})\tilde{U}_{j,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]}{\omega_{n,T}}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i})A_{j,i}^{\top}}{\omega_{n,T}}\right]\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}A_{j,i^{\prime}}=\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}c_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i^{\prime}},

where
c_{j,i}\equiv\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i})A_{j,i}/\omega_{n,T}]
. Let
C_{j,i}\equiv\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}>i\}}c_{j,i^{\prime}}
. Then the above
expression implies that

\frac{\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}+\tilde{V}_{i})\tilde{U}_{j,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]}{n\omega_{n,T}^{2}}=\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{C_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2}n\omega_{n,T}}.

(S726)

Since
\mathbb{E}\left[A_{j,i}\right]=0
and
A_{j,i}
are independent
across
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{C_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}n\omega_{n,T}T^{1/2}}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{C_{j,i}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]C_{j,i}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}^{2}n^{2}\omega_{n,T}^{2}T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\max_{i\leq n}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}])}{n^{2}\omega_{n,T}^{2}T}\sum_{i\leq n}n_{g_{j}(i)}^{-2}C_{j,i}^{\top}C_{j,i}.

(S727)

Applying Assumptions
1
,
9
and
12
(ii), and Rosenthal’s
inequality of strong mixing processes (e.g., (
S568
) with
\tilde{p}=4+\delta
in the proof of Lemma
S47
), we obtain

\max_{i\leq n}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}|^{4+\delta}]=\max_{i\leq n}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(T_{m}^{-1/2}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\tilde{\psi}_{j\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,g,m}^{\ast})\right)^{4+\delta}\right]\leq K.

(S728)

By Corollary A.2. in
26
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\max_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im^{\prime}}]\right|

(S729)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\max_{i\leq n}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}|^{2+\delta}])^{2}\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\left|\alpha_{i}(h)\right|^{\delta/(2+\delta)}\leq K,

where the second inequality is by Assumption
1
(iv) and (
S728
). In view of the upper bound in (
S729
),
we can use Geršgorin’s disc theorem (see, e.g., Theorem 6.1.1 in
27
) to show that

\max_{j=1,2}\max_{i\leq n}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}])\leq K.

(S730)

Using Lemma
S46
(with
X_{1}=(\tilde{\Psi}_{i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{i}^{\ast})/\omega_{n,T}
and
X_{2}=A_{j,i}
) and the inequality above
obtains

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\left\|c_{j,i}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]))\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}^{\ast}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}^{\ast}+\tilde{V}_{1,i}^{\ast}-\tilde{V}_{2,i}^{\ast})^{2}}{\omega_{n,T}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq K\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}^{\ast}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}^{\ast})^{2}}{\omega_{n,T}^{2}}\right]+K\frac{\max_{j=1,2}\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{V}_{j,i}^{\ast}|^{2}]}{\omega_{n,T}^{2}}.

(S731)

Applying Assumptions
1
and
12
(i), and Rosenthal’s inequality of
strong mixing processes, we can show that

\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}^{\ast}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}^{\ast}}{\omega_{n,T}}\right)^{4}\right]=\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\frac{\psi_{1}^{\ast}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i,t}^{\ast})-\psi_{2}^{\ast}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,i,t}^{\ast})}{\omega_{n,T}}\right)^{4}\right]\leq K,

(S732)

where
\psi_{j}^{\ast}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i,t}^{\ast})\equiv\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i,t}^{\ast})-\mathbb{E}[\psi_{j}(z_{i,t};\phi_{j,i,t}^{\ast})]
. Similarly for any
\delta^{\prime}\in[0,2+\delta/2]
,

\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{V}_{j,i}^{\ast}|^{2+\delta^{\prime}}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}])\right)^{2+\delta^{\prime}}\right]}{(2n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2})^{2+\delta^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{KM_{j}^{1+\delta^{\prime}/2}}{(n_{g_{j}(i)}T^{1/2})^{2+\delta^{\prime}}}.

(S733)

Therefore by (
S731
), (
S732
) and (
S733
) with
\delta^{\prime}=0
, and Assumptions
1
(i)
and
11
(i), we can deduce that

\max_{i\leq n}\left\|c_{j,i}\right\|^{2}\leq K\left(1+M_{j}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{g}^{-2}\right).

(S734)

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
S734
) yields

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\frac{C_{j,i}^{\top}C_{j,i}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\max_{i\leq n}\frac{\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\in\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}>i\}}|c_{j,i_{1}}^{\top}c_{j,i_{2}}|}{n_{g_{j}(i)}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}\left(\frac{\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{j}(i)}:\text{
}i^{\prime}>i\}}\left\|c_{j,i^{\prime}}\right\|}{n_{g_{j}(i)}}\right)^{2}\leq K\left(1+M_{j}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{g}^{-2}\right).

(S735)

Collecting the results in (
S727
), (
S730
)
and (
S735
), and applying Assumption
11
(i) obtains

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{C_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}n\omega_{n,T}T^{1/2}}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{Tn^{2}\omega_{n,T}^{2}}\sum_{i\leq n}\frac{C_{j,i}^{\top}C_{j,i}}{n_{g_{j}(i)}^{2}}\leq KT^{-1}\left(1+M_{j}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{g}^{-2}\right).

(S736)

The desired result in (
S725
) now follows from (
S726
), (
S736
), Assumption
10
(ii) and
Markov’s inequality.
Q.E.D.

Lemma S70
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
A4
,
we have

(n\omega_{n,T}^{2})^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{i}^{2}]\right)=O_{p}\left(T^{-1/2}+\max_{j=1,2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(G_{j}n_{g})^{-1/2}\right).

(S737)

Proof of Lemma
S70
. Since

\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{U}_{i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{U}_{1,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{U}_{2,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right],

the claim of the lemma follows if

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{j,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{j,i}^{2}]\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(T^{-1/2}+\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(G_{j}n_{g})^{-1/2}\right),\text{ \ and}

(S738)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}]\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(T^{-1/2}+\max_{j=1,2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}(G_{j}n_{g})^{-1/2}\right).

(S739)

We next verify (
S738
) and (
S739
).

We first show (
S738
) for
j=1
. The proof for
j=2
follows
the same arguments. For simplicity of notations, we suppose that for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
I_{g}=\{N_{g}+1,\ldots,N_{g}+n_{g}\}
where
N_{g}\equiv\sum_{g^{\prime}<g}n_{g^{\prime}}
. Following these notations,
for any
i\in I_{g}
and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
we can write

\tilde{U}_{1,i}=(n_{g}T^{1/2})^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}}.

Since
\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i^{\prime}}
is non-random
given
\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}
and
A_{1,i}
is independent of
\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i^{\prime}}
, for any
i\in I_{g}

and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle n_{g}^{2}T\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}])A_{1,i_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}])A_{1,i^{\prime}}+2\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+2}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{2}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{1}-1}A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}])A_{1,i_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}]\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}+2\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+2}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{2}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{1}-1}A_{1,i_{2}}A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}\right)\right),

which implies that

\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i}}{n_{g}^{2}T}+2\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}}}{n_{g}^{2}T},

(S740)

where

D_{1,i}\equiv\sum_{i^{\prime}=i+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}]

for any
i\in I_{g}
. Since
\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}}]=0
for any
i,i^{\prime}\in I_{g}
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
, by (
S740
) and the law of iterated expectation,

\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}^{2}]=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i}]}{n_{g}^{2}T}.

(S741)

By (
S740
) and (
S741
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{j,i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{j,i}^{2}]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i}-\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i}]}{n_{g}^{2}T}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ }+2\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}}}{n_{g}^{2}T}.

(S742)

In view of the decomposition in (
S742
), it is clear that (
S738
) holds for
j=1
by Markov’s inequality if

\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i}}{n_{g}^{2}T}\right)\leq KG_{1}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}

(S743)

and

\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}}}{n_{g}^{2}T}\right)\leq KT^{-1}.

(S744)

We next prove (
S743
) and (
S744
).

First note that the upper bound on the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]
established in (
S730
) implies that

\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i}]=\max_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}])\leq M_{j}\max_{i\leq n}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}])\leq KM_{j},

(S745)

where the first inequality is because
\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}])
is equal to the sum of the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]
, which is bounded by
M_{j}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}])
. Also in (
S733
), we have shown
that

\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(M_{j}^{-1/2}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}])\right)^{2}\right]\leq K.

(S746)

By (
S745
) and (
S746
), and the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[(A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{i\leq n}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}])\right)^{2}\right]+\left(\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{j}}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}^{2}]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq KM_{j}+\max_{i\leq n}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i}])^{2}\leq KM_{j}^{2}.

(S747)

By (
S730
), we also have

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\frac{\lambda_{\max}(D_{1,i})}{n_{g}}=\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}=i+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}])}{n_{g}}\leq K.

(S748)

Getting back to (
S743
), we note that since
A_{1,i}
are
independent across
i
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i}}{n_{g}^{2}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i})}{\left(n_{g}^{2}T\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i})^{2}]}{\left(n_{g}^{2}T\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{(\lambda_{\max}(D_{1,i}))^{2}\mathbb{E}[(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i})^{2}]}{\left(n_{g}^{2}T\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+1}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}(n_{g}T)^{-2}M_{1}^{2}\leq KM_{1}^{2}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}^{2}G_{1}T^{-2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\leq KG_{1}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}

(S749)

where the third inequality is by (
S747
) and (
S748
), and the last inequality is by (
S547
). This
establishes the desired result in (
S743
).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}}}{n_{g}^{2}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}})}{(n_{g}^{2}T)^{2}}=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(A_{1,i}^{\top}D_{1,i}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{2}]}{(n_{g}^{2}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{(\lambda_{\max}(D_{1,i}))^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}])\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i}]}{(n_{g}^{2}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}-1}\frac{M_{1}}{n_{g}T^{2}}\leq KM_{1}G_{1}T^{-2}\leq KT^{-1},

(S750)

where the second inequality is by (
S730
) and (
S748
), and the fourth inequality is by Assumptions
1
(i)
and
10
(ii). This shows (
S744
).

To derive (
S739
), we use the same notation for the
structure in
\mathcal{G}_{1}
employed in verifying (
S738
)
for
j=1
above. That is for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
I_{g}=\{N_{g}+1,\ldots,N_{g}+n_{g}\}
where
N_{g}\equiv\sum_{g^{\prime}<g}n_{g^{\prime}}
.
Moreover, for each
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
, we order
I_{g}
according to the
group identity
g_{2}(i)
such that
g_{2}(i)
is non-decreasing. Based on
the values of
g_{2}(i)
, we can divide
I_{g}
to
M_{g}
subsets (
1\leq M_{g}\leq G_{2}
):
I_{g,l}\equiv\{i_{g,l-1}+1,\ldots,i_{g,l}\}
(
l=1,\ldots,M_{g}
) with
i_{g,0}\equiv N_{g}
,
i_{g,M_{g}}\equiv N_{g}+n_{g}
and
i_{g,j}\in I_{g}
for
j=1,\ldots,M_{g}
such that
g_{2}(i)=g_{2}(i^{\prime})
for any
i,i^{\prime}\in I_{g,l}
. For each
i
,
let

Q_{1,i}\equiv\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{2}(i)}\text{: }i^{\prime}\leq N_{g_{1}(i)}\}\text{ \ and \ }Q_{2,i}\equiv\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{2}(i)}\text{: }N_{g_{1}(i)}+1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i-1\}.

By definition,
Q_{1,i}
includes the individuals who share the same group
identity with individual
i
under model 2 but are in groups
I_{g^{\prime}}

with
g^{\prime}<g_{1}(i)
under model 1. On the other hand,
Q_{2,i}

includes the individuals who share the same group identity with individual
i
under model 2, and are in the same group
g_{1}(i)
of individual
i

under model 1 but have their index strictly less than
i
. Since
N_{g_{1}(i)}\leq i-1
, it is clear that
Q_{1,i}
and
Q_{2,i}
are mutually
exclusive, and
\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{2}(i)}
:
i^{\prime}<i\}=Q_{1,i}\cup Q_{2,i}
.

Using the above notations, for any
i\in I_{g}
and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
we can write

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}

\displaystyle=(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T)^{-1}\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}}\right)\left(\sum_{\{i^{\prime}\in I_{g_{2}(i)}:\text{ }i^{\prime}<i\}}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i}}\frac{A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}A_{1,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i_{2}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}+\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{2,i}}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}A_{2,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}.

(S751)

Let
d_{12,i}\equiv\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]
and
d_{21,i}\equiv d_{12,i}^{\top}
. Then by the independence of
A_{j,i}
across
i
, we
obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ }+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{2,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}.

(S752)

To study the two terms in the RHS of (
S752
), we first
investigate
d_{12,i}
. Applying Lemma
S46
(with
X_{1}=A_{1,i}
and
X_{2}=A_{2,i}
) yields

\max_{i\leq n}\lambda_{\max}(d_{12,i}d_{21,i})\leq\max_{i\leq n}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}])\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}])\leq K,

(S753)

where the second inequality is by (
S730
).

Consider any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
. Since
I_{g}=\cup_{l\leq M_{g}}I_{g,l}
,
and for any
i\in I_{g,l}
,
Q_{1,i}=Q_{1,i_{g,l}}
and
n_{g_{2}(i)}=n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}
, we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}.

(S754)

By the definition of
I_{g,l}
,

\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}=\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}}d_{21,i}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i_{1}}=D_{1,g}^{l}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}A_{1,i_{1}}+\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}},

where
D_{1,g}^{l}\equiv\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}}d_{21,i}
and
D_{2,gi}^{l}\equiv\sum_{i^{\prime}=i+1}^{i_{g,l}}d_{21,i^{\prime}}
. Hence
the summation in the RHS of the second equality of (
S754
)
can be further decomposed into two terms which leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{1,g}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ }+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}.

(S755)

For any
T\times 1
real vector
a
, (
S753
) and the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle a^{\top}(D_{1,g}^{l})^{\top}D_{1,g}^{l}a

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}}a^{\top}d_{12,i_{1}^{\prime}}d_{21,i_{2}^{\prime}}a\leq\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}}\left\|a^{\top}d_{12,i^{\prime}}\right\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}\lambda_{\max}(d_{12,i}d_{21,i})\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}}(a^{\top}a)^{1/2}\right)^{2}\leq K(a^{\top}a)(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{2},

which implies that

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{l\leq M_{g}}\max_{i\in I_{g,l}}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{1,g}^{l})^{\top}D_{1,g}^{l})}{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{2}}\leq K.

(S756)

Similarly, we can show that

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{l\leq M_{g}}\max_{i\in I_{g,l}}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{2,gi}^{l})^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l})}{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{2}}\leq K.

(S757)

By the independence of
A_{1,i}
and
A_{2,i}
across
i
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{1,g}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(D_{1,g}^{l})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i_{2}}A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}]D_{1,g}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}]}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]))^{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{1,g}^{l})^{\top}D_{1,g}^{l})M_{1}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{2}(i_{g,l-1}-N_{g})(\#Q_{1,i_{g,l}})}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1}}{n_{g}}\leq KG_{1}M_{1}T^{-2}\leq KT^{-1}

(S758)

where the
\#Q_{1,i_{g,l}}
denotes the cardinality of
Q_{1,i_{g,l}}
, the
second inequality is by (
S730
) and (
S756
), and the third inequality is by
n_{g}\geq i_{g,l-1}-N_{g}
and
n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}\geq\#Q_{1,i_{g,l}}+i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1}
such that
(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})(\#Q_{1,i_{g,l}})n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}^{-2}\leq 1
, and the
fourth inequality is due to
\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})=n_{g}
,
and the last inequality is by
G_{1}M_{1}T^{-1}=G_{1}M_{1}(nT)^{-1/2}(n/T)^{1/2}\leq K
where the
inequality holds by Assumptions
1
(i) and
10
(ii). Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i_{2}}A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}]D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}]}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]))^{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i_{g,l}}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l})^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l})M_{1}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{2}(i_{g,l-1}-N_{g})(\#Q_{1,i_{g,l}})}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})})^{2}}\leq KG_{1}M_{1}T^{-2}\leq KT^{-1}.

(S759)

Since
\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}]}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}=0
, by (
S755
), (
S758
), (
S759
) and Markov’s
inequality, we have

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{1,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(S760)

We next study the second term in the RHS of (
S752
). For
any
i\in I_{g,l}
, by the arrangement in
I_{g}
, it is clear that
Q_{2,i}=\{i_{g,l-1}+1,\ldots,i-1\}
and hence

\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{2,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}=\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}+\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}.

(S761)

Since

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}d_{12,i}A_{2,i_{2}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i_{g,l}}d_{12,i}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}A_{2,i_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(D_{2,gi_{2}}^{l})^{\top}A_{2,i_{2}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T},

(S762)

by the similar arguments for showing (
S758
), we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\frac{A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}d_{12,i}A_{2,i_{2}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(D_{2,gi_{2}}^{l})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i_{2}}A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}]D_{2,gi_{2}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}]}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]))^{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i_{g,l-1}}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{2,gi_{2}}^{l})^{\top}D_{2,gi_{2}}^{l})M_{1}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{3}(i_{g,l-1}-N_{g})}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1}}{n_{g}}\leq KG_{1}M_{1}T^{-2}\leq KT^{-1}.

(S763)

Next note that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{1}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}+2\sum_{i\in I_{g,l}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i-1}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{A_{2,i}^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l}A_{1,i}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}+2\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i_{g,l}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}.

(S764)

By the independence of
A_{1\gamma,i}
and
A_{2\gamma,i}
across
i
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{A_{2,i}^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l}A_{1,i}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(A_{2,i}^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l}A_{1,i})}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(A_{2,i}^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l}A_{1,i})^{2}]}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(A_{1,i}^{\top}(D_{2,gi}^{l})^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l}A_{1,i})(A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i})]}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{2,gi}^{l})^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l})(\mathbb{E}[||A_{1,i}||^{4}]\mathbb{E}[||A_{2,i}||^{4}])^{1/2}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{3}M_{1}M_{2}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}\leq KM_{1}M_{2}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\leq KG_{2}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1},

(S765)

where the second inequality is by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the third
inequality is by Hölder’s inequality, the fourth inequality is by (
S747
) and (
S757
), and the last inequality
is by Assumptions
1
(i) and
10
(ii). By the similar arguments for
showing (
S758
), we also have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i_{g,l}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i_{g,l}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}})}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i_{g,l}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i_{1}}^{\top}(D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i_{2}}A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}]D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l}A_{1,i_{1}}]}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}A_{j,i}^{\top}]))^{2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i_{1}=i_{g,l-1}+2}^{i_{g,l}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{1}-1}\frac{\lambda_{\max}((D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l})^{\top}D_{2,gi_{1}}^{l})M_{1}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}T^{-2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\frac{(i_{g,l}-i_{g,l-1})^{4}}{(n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})})^{2}}\leq KG_{1}M_{1}T^{-2}\leq KT^{-1}.

(S766)

Collecting the results in (
S761
)-(
S766
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{i_{2}\in Q_{2,i}}\sum_{i_{1}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{A_{2,i_{2}}^{\top}d_{21,i}A_{1,i_{1}}}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i)}T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{l\leq M_{g}}\sum_{i=i_{g,l-1}+1}^{i_{g,l}-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}^{\top}D_{2,gi}^{l}A_{1,i}]}{n_{g}n_{g_{2}(i_{g,l})}T}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ }+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}+G_{2}^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1/2}),

which together with (
S752
) and (
S760
)
implies

\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}])=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}+G_{2}^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1/2}).

(S767)

We can switch the role of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
, and use
the same argument above to show that

\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}|\mathcal{F}_{nT,i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{1,i}\tilde{U}_{2,i}])=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}+G_{1}^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{g}^{-1/2}).

(S768)

Since

\displaystyle\min\left\{G_{1}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{g}^{-1},\text{\ }G_{2}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right\}\leq\left(G_{1}^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{g}^{-1/2}\right)\left(G_{2}^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{G_{1}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}+G_{2}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}n_{g}^{-1}}{2}\leq\max_{j=1,2}\left(G_{j}^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}n_{g}^{-1}\right),

(S769)

the desired result in (
S739
) follows from (
S767
), (
S768
) and (
S769
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma S71
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
A4
,
we have for
j=1,2

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{j}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|M_{j}^{-1/2}(n_{g}T)^{1/2}\tilde{U}_{j,i}\right|^{4}\right]\leq K.

(S770)

Proof of Lemma
S71
. We only prove (
S770
) for
j=1
, since the proof for
j=2
follows the same
arguments. We shall use the structure of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
employed in
verifying (
S738
) for
j=1
in the proof of Lemma
S70
. That is for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
I_{g}=\{N_{g}+1,\ldots,N_{g}+n_{g}\}
where
N_{g}\equiv\sum_{g^{\prime}<g}n_{g^{\prime}}
.
Following these notations, we can write

\tilde{U}_{1,i}=(n_{g}T^{1/2})^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}}

for any
i\in I_{g}
and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
. Since
\{A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i^{\prime}}\}_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i-1}
are independent conditioning on
A_{j,i}
, we can use Rosenthal’s inequality of independent random variables
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(n_{g}T^{1/2}\tilde{U}_{1,i})^{4}|A_{1,i}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}}\right)^{4}\right|A_{1,i}\right]

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ }\leq K\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{2}\right|A_{1,i}\right]\right)^{2}+K\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{4}\right|A_{1,i}\right].

(S771)

By the independence of
A_{j,i}
and
A_{j,i^{\prime}}
for any
i\neq i^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{2}\right|A_{1,i}\right]\overset{}{=}A_{1,i}^{\top}\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}]\right)A_{1,i}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ }\overset{}{\leq}(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i})\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}])\text{ }\overset{}{\leq}Kn_{g}(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i}),

(S772)

where the second inequality is by (
S730
). Therefore,

\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{2}\right|A_{1,i}\right]\right)^{2}\right]\leq K\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i})^{2}]\leq KM_{1}^{2},

(S773)

where the second inequality is by (
S747
).

For any
i,i^{\prime}=1,\ldots,n
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
, by the
independence between
A_{j,i}
and
A_{j,i^{\prime}}
, and the triangle
inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i^{\prime}m}\right|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}}|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i^{\prime}m_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i^{\prime}m_{4}}]|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}}|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i^{\prime}m_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i^{\prime}m_{4}}]|\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}}|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\leq n}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}|^{4}]\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}}|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]|

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}}|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]|,

(S774)

where the third inequality is by Hölder’s inequality and the last
inequality is by (
S728
). Consider any
m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}
from
\mathcal{M}_{1}
with
m_{1}\leq\ldots\leq m_{4}
. Let
r\equiv\max_{j=1,2,3}\{m_{j+1}-m_{j}\}
and
j_{r}\equiv\min\{j=1,2,3:m_{j+1}-m_{j}=r\}
. It is clear that
r
ranges
from
0
to
M_{1}-1
. When
r=0
, we have
m_{1}=\ldots=m_{4}=m
for some
m\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
and in this case

|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]|=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}^{4}]\leq K,

(S775)

where the inequality is by (
S728
). We next consider the
case that
r=1,\ldots,M_{1}-1
. By Hölder’s inequality and Corollary
A.2. in
26
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{j_{r}}}]\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{j_{r}+1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha_{i}(r))^{\delta/(4+\delta)}\left\|\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{j_{r}}}\right\|_{(4+\delta)/j_{r}}\left\|\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{j_{r}+1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}\right\|_{(4+\delta)/(4-j_{r})}

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha_{i}(r))^{\delta/(4+\delta)}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\left\|\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}\right\|_{(4+\delta)}^{4}\leq K(\alpha_{i}(r))^{\delta/(4+\delta)},

(S776)

where the last inequality is by (
S728
). Since
\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}]=0
, by (
S776
) we have

\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]\right|\leq K(\alpha_{i}(r))^{\delta/(4+\delta)}

(S777)

if
j_{r}=1
or
3
. On the other hand if
j_{r}=2
, then

\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\cdots\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]\right|\leq K(\alpha_{i}(r))^{\delta/(4+\delta)}+\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}]\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]\right|.

(S778)

Since for each
r_{0}=1,\ldots,M_{1}-1
, there are at most
3(M_{1}-r_{0})(1+r_{0})^{2}
of indices
m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}
satisfying

1\leq m_{1}\leq\ldots\leq m_{4}\leq M_{1}
such that
\max_{j=1,2,3}\{m_{j+1}-m_{j}\}=r_{0}
. From (
S775
), (
S776
) and (
S777
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}\leq\ldots\leq m_{4}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}^{4}]+K\sum_{r_{0}=1}^{M_{1}-1}(\alpha_{i}(r_{0}))^{\delta/(4+\delta)}(M_{1}-r_{0})(1+r_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ }+\sum_{m_{1}\leq\ldots\leq m_{4}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}]\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{3}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{4}}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}+KM_{1}\sum_{r_{0}=1}^{M_{1}-1}(\alpha_{i}(r_{0}))^{\delta/(4+\delta)}r_{0}^{2}+\left(\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{1}}\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im_{2}}]\right|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq KM_{1}+KM_{1}^{2}\leq KM_{1}^{2},

(S779)

where the third inequality is by Assumption
1
(iv) and (
S729
). Since for any
m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
there are at most 24 ways to order them from the smallest to the
largest, by (
S774
) and (
S779
), we deduce
that
\mathbb{E}[(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{4}]\leq KM_{1}^{2}
for
any
i\neq i^{\prime}
, which implies that

\max_{i\leq n}n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[(M_{1}^{-1/2}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{4}\right]\leq K.

(S780)

Collecting the results in (
S771
), (
S773
), (
S780
), we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[|M_{1}^{-1/2}(n_{g}T)^{1/2}\tilde{U}_{1,i}|^{4}]

\displaystyle=\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}\left[n_{g}^{-2}\mathbb{E}[(M_{1}^{-1/2}n_{g}T^{1/2}\tilde{U}_{1,i})^{4}|A_{1,i}]\right]

\displaystyle\leq K\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.(M_{1}^{-1/2}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{2}\right|A_{1,i}\right]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ }+K\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\max_{i\in I_{g}}\left(n_{g}^{-2}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}\left[(M_{1}^{-1/2}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})^{4}\right]\right)\overset{}{\leq}K,

which shows (
S770
) for
j=1
.
Q.E.D.

Appendix G
Further Examples of Linear Models

In this section, we provide several examples from the linear panel model

y_{i,t}=x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta+u_{i,t}+\varepsilon_{i,t},

where
y_{i,t}
is the dependent variable,
x_{i,t}
are the observed
regressors,
u_{i,t}
is a unobservable term which may include individual
and/or time fixed effects, and
\varepsilon_{i,t}
is i.i.d. across
i
and

t
and normally distributed with mean zero and variance
\sigma^{2}
. The
data are
\{z_{i,t}\}_{i\leq n,t\leq T}
, where
z_{it}=(y_{i,t},x_{i,t}^{\top})^{\top}
. As illustrated in the examples below,
u_{i,t}
takes
different forms under various model specifications.

Example 1. (Time Invariant Fixed Effect)

In this model,
u_{i,t}
includes a time invariant fixed effect, i.e.,
u_{i,t}=\gamma_{i}
. Since there is a group structure in
t
and no group
structure in
i
, we have
\mathcal{G}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
I_{g}=\{g\}
for
any
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{M}=\{1\}
and
I_{m}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
for
m\in\mathcal{M}
. Therefore
\gamma_{g,1}=\gamma_{g}\
for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
, and the log-likelihood function for any
i=g
and any
t=m
is

\log\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta-\gamma_{g}\right)^{2},

which leads to the joint-likelihood function

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{nT\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\theta-\gamma_{i}\right)^{2},

where
\theta=(\beta^{\top},\sigma^{2})^{\top}
.

Example 2. (Time Varying Fixed Effect)

Consider the time variate fixed effect model where
u_{i,t}=\gamma_{i,1}

for
t=1,\ldots,T_{0}
and
u_{i,t}=\gamma_{i,2}
for
t=T_{0}+1,\ldots,T
. In this case, there is no group structure along
i
but a group structure
on
t
. Hence we have
\mathcal{G}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
I_{g}=\{g\}
for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{M}=\{1,2\}
and

I_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\{1,\ldots,T_{0}\},&\text{if }m=1\\
\{T_{0}+1,\ldots,T\},&\text{if }m=2\end{array}\right..

Therefore the log-likelihood function for any
i=g
and any
t\in I_{m}
is

\log f\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta-\gamma_{g,m}\right)^{2}

which leads to the joint-likelihood function

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{nT\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{m\in\{1,2\}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta-\gamma_{im}\right)^{2},

where
\theta=(\beta^{\top},\sigma^{2})^{\top}
.

Example 3. (Time Fixed Effect)

In this example, only the time fixed effect shows up in
u_{i,t}
, i.e.,
u_{i,t}=\zeta_{t}
for any
i
and any
t
. Since there is no grouping
structure in
t
and a group structure in
i
, we have
\mathcal{G}=\{1\}
,
I_{g}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{M}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
and
I_{m}=\{m\}
for
m\in\mathcal{M}
. Therefore
\gamma_{1,m}=\zeta_{m}\
for any
m\in\mathcal{M}
, and the log-likelihood
function for any
i=g
and any
t=m
is

\log\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta-\zeta_{m}\right)^{2},

which leads to the joint-likelihood function

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{nT\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\theta-\zeta_{t}\right)^{2},

where
\theta=(\beta^{\top},\sigma^{2})^{\top}
.

Example 4. (Grouped Time Fixed Effects)

In this example, there is a group structure in
i
but no group structure in

t
, i.e.,
u_{i,t}=\gamma_{g\left(i\right),t}
, where
g\left(\cdot\right)
is a known function with range
\{1,\ldots,G\}
. Hence in this
case,
\mathcal{G}=\{1,\ldots,G\}
,
I_{g}=\{g(i)=g:i=1,\ldots,n\}
for
any
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{M}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
and
I_{m}=\{m\}
for
m\in\mathcal{M}
.
9
investigated this model where
the function
g\left(\cdot\right)
was considered unknown, and one of
their goals was to estimate the latent group structure that is implied by
g\left(\cdot\right)
. The log-likelihood function for any
i\in I_{g}

and any
t=m\
is

\log\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta-\gamma_{g,t}\right)^{2}

which leads to the joint-likelihood function

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi\left(z_{it};\theta,\gamma_{g,m}\right)=-\frac{nT\log(\sigma^{2})}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(y_{i,t}-x_{i,t}^{\top}\beta-\gamma_{g,t}\right)^{2},

where
\theta=(\beta^{\top},\sigma^{2})^{\top}
.

Below, we present an expansion of the likelihood function and discuss
Assumption
10
.

Example 1. (Time Invariant Fixed Effect ctd.)
. In this
example, the expansion of the likelihood function can be written as

\sum_{i\leq n}\hat{\Psi}_{i}(\hat{\phi}_{i})=\sum_{i\leq n}\hat{\Psi}_{i}(\phi_{i}^{\ast})-2^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,i}^{2}/\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,i}+o_{p}\left(n^{1/2}T^{-1}\right),

where
\phi_{i}\equiv(\theta^{\top},\gamma_{i})^{\top}
,
\hat{\Psi}_{i}(\phi_{i})\equiv T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i})
,
\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,i}\equiv T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
and
\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,i}\equiv T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma^{2}]
. Moreover we have
G=n
,
M=1
,
n_{g}=1
,
T_{m}=T
so Assumption
10
boils down to
n^{4/p}/T=o\left(1\right)

and
n/\left(nT\right)^{1/2}=\left(n/T\right)^{1/2}=O\left(1\right)
.

Example 2. (Time Varying Fixed Effect ctd.)
. In this
example, the expansion of the likelihood function can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}\hat{\Psi}_{im}(\hat{\phi}_{im})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}\hat{\Psi}_{im}(\phi_{im}^{\ast})

\displaystyle-2^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}T_{m}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{\top}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,im}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}+o_{p}\left((nM)^{1/2}\right),

where
\phi_{im}\equiv(\theta^{\top},\gamma_{im})^{\top}
,
\hat{\Psi}_{im}\equiv T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{im})
,
\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}\equiv T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{im}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
and
\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,im}\equiv T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{im}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma^{2}]
. Since
G=n
,
M=2
,
n_{g}=1
,
T_{1}=T_{0}
and
T_{2}=T-T_{0}
, Assumption
10

holds in this example if
n^{2/p}(T_{0}^{-1/2}+(T-T_{0})^{-1/2})=o(1)
and
n/\left(nT\right)^{1/2}=\left(n/T\right)^{1/2}=O\left(1\right)
.

Example 3. (Time Fixed Effect ctd.)
. In this example,
the expansion of the likelihood function can be written as

\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\Psi}_{t}(\hat{\phi}_{t})=\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\Psi}_{t}(\phi_{t}^{\ast})-2^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,t}^{2}/\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,t}+o_{p}\left(T^{1/2}n^{-1}\right),

where
\phi_{t}\equiv(\theta^{\top},\gamma_{t})^{\top}
,
\hat{\Psi}_{t}(\phi_{t})\equiv n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{t})
,
\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,t}\equiv n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{t}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
and
\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,t}\equiv n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{t}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma^{2}]
. In this case,
G=1
,
M=T
,
n_{g}=n
,
T_{m}=1
. Hence Assumption
10
becomes
T^{4/p}/n=o\left(1\right)
and
T/\left(nT\right)^{1/2}=\left(T/n\right)^{1/2}=O\left(1\right)
.

Example 4. (Grouped Time Fixed Effects ctd.)
. In this
example, the expansion of the likelihood function can be written as

\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}\hat{\Psi}_{gt}(\hat{\phi}_{gt})=\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}\hat{\Psi}_{gt}(\phi_{gt}^{\ast})-2^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}n_{g}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,gt}^{\top}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,gt}^{-1}\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,gt}+o_{p}\left((GT)^{1/2}\right)

where
\phi_{gt}\equiv(\theta^{\top},\gamma_{g(i)t})^{\top}
,
\hat{\Psi}_{gt}\equiv n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{gt}^{\ast})
,
\hat{\Psi}_{\gamma,gt}\equiv n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{gt}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
and
\Psi_{\gamma\gamma,im}\equiv n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{gt}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma^{2}]
. Since
M=T
,
n_{g}=\#I_{g}
where
\#I_{g}
denotes the cardinality of set
I_{g}
, and
T_{m}=1
, Assumption
10
holds in this example if
(GT)^{2/p}\max_{g\leq G}n_{g}^{-1/2}=o(1)
and
(GT)(nT)^{-1/2}=GT^{1/2}n^{-1/2}=O(1)
.

G.1
Time Invariant vs. Time Varying Individual Effects

Suppose that model 1 specifies a time invariant individual fixed effect with
joint likelihood function
\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{1}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{1},\gamma_{1,i}\right)
, and model 2 specifies a time
varying individual fixed effect with joint likelihood function
\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{2}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{2},\gamma_{2,im}\right)
where
M_{2}>1
is a fixed integer. In this
example, we have
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
for model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,M_{2}\}
for model 2.
25
25

25

Since
G_{2}=n
in this example, we let
M_{2}
be a fixed integer such that
Assumption
10
(ii) is satisfied.

We first provide the specific forms of
\tilde{V}_{j,i}
and
\tilde{U}_{j,i}
in this example. It is clear that

\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}=\frac{T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{1\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i}^{\ast})}{\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[-\psi_{1\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i}^{\ast})\right]\right)^{1/2}}

and

\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}=\frac{T_{m}^{-1/2}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{2\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,im}^{\ast})}{\left(T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[-\psi_{2\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,im}^{\ast})\right]\right)^{1/2}},

and we suppress the dependence of
\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}
on
m
since
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
. Therefore,

\tilde{V}_{1,i}=(2T^{1/2})^{-1}\text{\ }\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}\text{
, \ }\tilde{V}_{2,i}=(2T^{1/2})^{-1}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}\text{ \ and \ }\tilde{U}_{j,i}=0\text{ for }j=1,2\text{.}

The expansion of the QLR statistic in (
101
) thus implies that

\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}}{2T^{1/2}}\right)+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(S781)

Applying Theorem
A3
obtains the asymptotic normality for
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}
with variance

\omega_{n,T}^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}}{2T^{1/2}}\right).

In the case that
\psi_{1}\left(\cdot\right)
and
\psi_{2}\left(\cdot\right)
take the same function form (say
\psi\left(\cdot\right)
),
model 2 nests model 1 under the null, since
\phi_{1,i}^{\ast}=\phi_{2,im}^{\ast}
by the definitions of
\phi_{1,i}^{\ast}
and
\phi_{2,im}^{\ast}
and their identification conditions. Therefore, the
expansion of the QLR statistic in (
S781
) is reduced to

\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}=(4nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}\right)+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(S782)

Therefore in this scenario

\omega_{n,T}^{2}=(4nT)^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}\right),

(S783)

and Assumption
11
(i) holds as long as
n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2})
is bounded away from zero. We next provides the formula
of
\omega_{n,T}^{2}
in the simplified case that
\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i,t}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma^{2}]
is constant
for any
i
and any
t
, and
\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i,t}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
is independent with constant variance across
t
.

Lemma S72
.

Consider two models with
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
for model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,M_{2}\}
for model
2 where
M_{2}
is a fixed positive integer. Suppose that: (i)
\psi_{1}\left(\cdot\right)
and
\psi_{2}\left(\cdot\right)
take
the same form
\psi\left(\cdot\right)
; (ii)
\psi_{\gamma,i,t}\equiv\partial\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i,t}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma
is
independent across
t
with
\mathbb{E}[\psi_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}]<K
; (iii)
\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}\psi(z_{i,t};\phi_{i,t}^{\ast})/\partial\gamma^{2}]=\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}
where
\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}
is a nonzero
constant; and (iv)
\min_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}(T_{m}/T)\geq K^{-1}
. Then

\displaystyle\omega_{n,T}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,i}^{4}+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-2w_{m})n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,im}^{4}}{2T\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}

\displaystyle+O\left(T^{-1}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}T_{m}^{-1}\right),

(S784)

where
w_{m}\equiv T_{m}T^{-1}
,
\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,i}\equiv\sqrt{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}}
,
\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,im}\equiv\sqrt{T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}}
and
\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\equiv\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{\gamma,i,t})
.

When
\psi_{\gamma,i,t}
has constant variance across
t
, i.e.,
\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}=\sigma_{\gamma,i}^{2}
for some finite constant
\sigma_{\gamma,i}^{2}
, we have

n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,i}^{4}=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,im}^{4}=\sigma_{\gamma,i}^{4},

and the approximated expression of
\omega_{n,T}^{2}
in (
S784
) becomes

\omega_{n,T}^{2}=\frac{M_{2}-1}{2}\frac{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\sigma_{\gamma,i}^{4}}{T\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}+O\left(T^{-1}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}T_{m}^{-1}\right),

(S785)

where the second term in the RHS of (
S785
) is asymptotically
negligible if
\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}T_{m}^{-1}=o(1)
.

G.2
Homogeneous vs. Heterogeneous Time Effects

Suppose that model 1 specifies a group heterogeneous time effect with joint
likelihood function
\sum_{g=1}^{G_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{1}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{1},\gamma_{1,gt}\right)
where
G_{1}>1
is a
fixed integer, and model 2 specifies a homogeneous time fixed effect with
joint likelihood function
\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{2},\gamma_{2,t}\right)
. In this example, we have
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,G_{1}\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\ldots,T\}

for model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
for model 2.
26
26

26

Since
M_{1}=T
in this example, we let
G_{1}
be a fixed integer such that
Assumption
10
(ii) is satisfied.

Under the specifications of model 1 and model 2,

\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i,t}=\frac{\psi_{1\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i,t}^{\ast})}{\left(n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}\left[-\psi_{1\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i,t}^{\ast})\right]\right)^{1/2}}

and

\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,i,t}=\frac{\psi_{2\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,t}^{\ast})}{\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[-\psi_{2\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,t}^{\ast})\right]\right)^{1/2}}

where we write
\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,im}
as
\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,i,t}

since
\mathcal{M}_{j}=\{1\ldots,T\}
. Without loss of generality, we
suppose that for any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
I_{g}=\{N_{g}+1,\ldots,N_{g}+n_{g}\}
where
N_{g}\equiv\sum_{g^{\prime}<g}n_{g^{\prime}}
.
Then for any
i\in I_{g}
and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{1,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i}}{2n_{g}T^{1/2}}\text{, \ }\tilde{U}_{1,i}=\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}}}{n_{g}T^{1/2}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{2,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i}}{2nT^{1/2}}\text{ \ and \ }\tilde{U}_{2,i}=\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i-1}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i^{\prime}}}{nT^{1/2}},

where
A_{j,i}=(\tilde{\Psi}_{j\gamma,i,t})_{t\leq T}
for
j=1,2
.
27
27

27

Following the trandition that the summaion over an empty set is zero, we
have
\tilde{U}_{1,N_{g}+1}=0
and
\tilde{U}_{2,1}=0
in the above display.

Applying Theorem
A3
, we obtain the asymptotic normality for
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}
with variance

\omega_{n,T}=\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i}\right)}.

Moreover, the lemma below shows that
\tilde{V}_{j,i}
(
j=1,2
) are
asymptotically negligible, and hence the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}
is driven by
n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})
. Moreover, the variance of
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}
is given by

\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}=\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i})+\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})\right)}.

Hence Assumption
11
(i) holds as long as
\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})
is bounded away from zero.

Lemma S73
.

Consider two models with
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,G_{1}\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\ldots,T\}
for model 1 where
G_{1}
is
a fixed positive integer, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
for model 2. Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
(i) and
12
hold for models 1 and 2,
and
\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})>K^{-1}
.
Then

\frac{\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]-QLR_{n,T}^{\ast}}{\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}}\rightarrow_{d}N(0,1),

(S786)

where
\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]=(2nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\left(n_{g}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i}]-n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i}]\right)
, and

\displaystyle(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i})

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ }+\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\left\|\Sigma_{1\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}-2w_{g}\left\|\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}\right\|^{2})+\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}w_{g}\Sigma_{2\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}}{2nT}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ }+O\left(n^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right).

(S787)

where
w_{g}\equiv n_{g}n^{-1}
,
\Sigma_{j\gamma,g}\equiv n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[A_{j\gamma,i}A_{j\gamma,i}^{\top}]

and
\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}\equiv n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]
.

To get a more explicit form for
(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}
, we consider
the scenario that the conditions of Lemma
S72
hold. In this
case, model 1 nests model 1 under the null hypothesis and
\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i})=0
. Moreover,
\Sigma_{j\gamma,g}=\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}
with

\left\|\Sigma_{1\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}=\left\|\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}=\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{-2}\sum_{t\leq T}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,gt}^{4}\text{ \ \ and \ \ }\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}w_{g}\Sigma_{2\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}=\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{-2}\sum_{t\leq T}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,t}^{4}

where
\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,gt}\equiv\sqrt{n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}}
and
\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,t}^{2}\equiv\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}}
. Hence the expression
in (
S787
) leads to

(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}=\frac{T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,t}^{4}+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}(1-2w_{g})T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,gt}^{4}}{2n\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}+O\left(n^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right).

G.3
Individual vs. Time Fixed Effect Models

Suppose that model 1 specifies individual fixed effects with joint
likelihood
\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{1}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{1},\gamma_{1,i}\right)
, while model 2 specifies time
fixed effects with joint likelihood
\sum_{i\leq n}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{2}\left(z_{i,t};\theta_{1},\gamma_{2,t}\right)
. In this case,
we have
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
for
model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}

for model 2.

In this example, it is clear that

\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}=\frac{T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\psi_{1\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i}^{\ast})}{\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[-\psi_{1\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{1,i}^{\ast})\right]\right)^{1/2}}\text{\ \ and \ \ }\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,i,t}=\frac{\psi_{2\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,t}^{\ast})}{\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[-\psi_{2\gamma\gamma}(z_{i,t};\phi_{2,t}^{\ast})\right]\right)^{1/2}}

where we suppress the dependence of
\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,im}
on
m

since
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
, and write
\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}
as
\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,i,t}
since
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
.
Therefore,

\tilde{V}_{1,i}=\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}}{2T^{1/2}}\text{, \ }\tilde{U}_{1,i}=0\text{, \ }\tilde{V}_{2,i}=\frac{A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i}}{2nT^{1/2}}\text{ \ and \ }\tilde{U}_{2,i}=\frac{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i-1}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i^{\prime}}}{nT^{1/2}},

where
A_{2,i}=(\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,i,t})_{t\leq T}
.
28
28

28

Following the trandition that the summaion over an empty set is zero, we
have
\tilde{U}_{2,1}=0
in the above display.
Applying Theorem
A3
, we obtain the asymptotic normality for
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}

with variance

\omega_{n,T}=\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i}\right)}.

Since
\tilde{V}_{2,i}
is the same as the one in the Example 2, we can show
that it is asymptotically negligible and the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}
is driven by
n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})
. As we shall prove in
the lemma below, the variance of
\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}
in this example is
given by

\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}=\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i})+\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{2,i})\right)}.

Hence Assumption
11
(i) holds as long as
\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{2,i})
is bounded away from zero.

Lemma S74
.

Consider two models with
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
for model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
for model 2. Suppose
that Assumptions
1
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
(i) and
12
hold for models 1 and 2, and
\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{2,i})>K^{-1}
. Then

\frac{\widehat{QLR}_{n,T}-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]-QLR_{n,T}^{\ast}}{\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}}\rightarrow_{d}N(0,1),

(S788)

where
\mathbb{E}[S_{n,T}]=(2nT)^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}]-n^{-1}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i}])
, and

(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i})+\frac{\left\|n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]\right\|^{2}}{2nT}+O(n^{-2}).

(S789)

G.4
Proofs for Lemmas
S72
,
S73

and
S74

Proof of Lemma
S72
. Recall that in the two
models investigated in this lemma, we have
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,n\}

and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
for model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,M_{2}\}
for model 2 where
M_{2}

is a fixed positive integer. Under Conditions (i, iii) of the Lemma, we can
write

\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}=\frac{\left(\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}(T_{m}/T)^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}\right)^{2}}{-\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}}=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}\frac{T_{m}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2}}{-T\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}}+2\sum_{m=2}^{M_{2}}\frac{\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{m-1}T_{m}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}T_{m^{\prime}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im^{\prime}}}{-T\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}}

and
\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}=\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2}/(-\Psi_{\gamma\gamma})
for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
and any
i=1,\ldots,n
.
Therefore,

\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-T_{m}/T)\frac{\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2}}{\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}}-2\sum_{m=2}^{M_{2}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{m-1}\frac{T_{m}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}T_{m^{\prime}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im^{\prime}}}{T\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}}.

(S790)

Under Condition (ii) of the lemma,
\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}
are
independent across
m
for any
i
, which together with the expression in (
S790
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}\right)=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-T_{m}/T)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2})}{\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ }+4\sum_{m=2}^{M_{2}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{m-1}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(T_{m}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im})\mathrm{Var}(T_{m^{\prime}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im^{\prime}})}{T^{2}\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}.

(S791)

By Condition (ii) of the lemma,
\mathrm{Var}(T_{m}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im})=\sum_{t\in I\,_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}
for any
m
,
and hence

\displaystyle 4T^{-2}\sum_{m=2}^{M_{2}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{m-1}\mathrm{Var}(T_{m}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im})\mathrm{Var}(T_{m^{\prime}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=4T^{-2}\sum_{m=2}^{M_{2}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{m-1}\left(\sum_{t\in I\,_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{t\in I\,_{m^{\prime}}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=2\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}-2\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}w_{m}^{2}\left(T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I\,_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}.

(S792)

Without loss of generality for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
, let
I_{m}=\{t_{m,1},\ldots,t_{m,T_{m}}\}
. Then

\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2}=T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\psi_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}+2T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{j=2}^{T_{m}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j-1}\psi_{\gamma,i,t_{m,j}}\psi_{\gamma,i,t_{m,j^{\prime}}}.

Let
\kappa_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}\equiv\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{\gamma,i,t}^{2})
.
By Condition (ii) of the lemma

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2})

\displaystyle=T_{m}^{-2}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{\gamma,i,t}^{2})+4T_{m}^{-2}\sum_{j=2}^{T_{m}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j}\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{\gamma,i,t_{m,j}})\mathrm{Var}(\psi_{\gamma,i,t_{m,j^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=T_{m}^{-2}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\kappa_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}+2T_{m}^{-2}\left[\left(\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}-\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}\right],

which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-T_{m}/T)^{2}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{\gamma,im}^{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-w_{m})^{2}T_{m}^{-2}\left(\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\kappa_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}-2\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ \ }+2\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-w_{m})^{2}\left(T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}.

(S793)

Combining the results in (
S791
), (
S792
) and (
S793
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left[\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-2w_{m})\left(T_{m}^{-1}\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]}{\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-w_{m})^{2}T_{m}^{-2}\left(\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\kappa_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}-2\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}\right)}{\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}.

(S794)

Since
M_{2}
is fixed, by
\mathbb{E}[\psi_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}]<K
,

\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-w_{m})^{2}T_{m}^{-2}\left(\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\kappa_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}+2\sum_{t\in I_{m}}\sigma_{\gamma,i,t}^{4}\right)\leq KM_{2}\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}T_{m}^{-1}=O\left(\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}T_{m}^{-1}\right),

which together with (
S794
) and the definitions of
\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,i}
and
\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,im}
yields

\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{\Psi}_{1\gamma,i}^{2}-\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\tilde{\Psi}_{2\gamma,im}^{2}\right)=\frac{2\left(\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,i}^{4}+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{2}}(1-2w_{m})\sum_{i\leq n}\bar{\sigma}_{\gamma,im}^{4}\right)}{\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}^{2}}+O\left(\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}T_{m}^{-1}\right).

The claim in (
S784
) now follows from (
S783
) and the
above equation.
Q.E.D.

Proof of Lemma
S73
. Recall that in the two
models investigated in this lemma, we have
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,G_{1}\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\ldots,T\}
for model 1 where
G_{1}
is
a fixed positive integer, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
for model 2. First notice that by
G_{1}<K
,
G_{2}=1

and
M_{1}=M_{2}=T
, Assumption
10
(ii) holds. By the independence of
\{z_{i,t}\}
across
i
for all
t
, we
\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}
is uncorrelated with
\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i}
for any
i
.
Therefore,

\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})=\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i})+\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i}).

(S795)

The condition
\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})>K^{-1}
maintained in the lemma thus implies that

n(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}>K^{-1}.

(S796)

We first show (
S786
). Since
\tilde{V}_{j,i}
are independent
across
i
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\left(\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i}\right)\right)=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}n_{g}^{-2}\mathrm{Var}\left(T^{-1/2}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i}\right)+n^{-3}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(T^{-1/2}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K\left(n^{-2}+n^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right)\leq Kn^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}

(S797)

where the second inequality is by (
S747
) (where we show
that
\mathrm{Var}(M_{j}^{-1/2}A_{j,i}^{\top}A_{j,i})\leq K
with
M_{j}=T

here). Next note that

\displaystyle\omega_{n,T}^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})+n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i})

\displaystyle+2n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Cov}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i},\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i})

\displaystyle=(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}+2n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Cov}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i},\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i})+O\left(n^{-1}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right),

(S798)

where the second equality is by (
S797
). By Hölder’s
inequality,

\displaystyle\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Cov}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i},\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i}))^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})\right)^{1/2}\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i})\right)^{1/2},

which together with the definition of
\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}
, (
S795
) and (
S797
) implies that

\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Cov}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{U}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i},\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{V}_{2,i})\right|=O\left(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}n^{-1/2}\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1/2}\right).

(S799)

By (
S796
), (
S798
) and (
S799
), we can
deduce that

\frac{\omega_{n,T}^{2}}{(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}}=1+O\left(\frac{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1/2}}{n^{1/2}\omega_{n,T}^{\ast}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}}{n(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}}\right)=1+O\left(\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1/2}\right).

(S800)

The claim in (
S786
) thus follows from Theorem
A3
and
Theorem
A4
, Assumption
12
(iii) and Slutsky’s theorem.

We next turn to the proof of (
S787
). Since
\mathbb{E}[A_{j,i}]=0

and
A_{j,i}
are independent across
i
, for any
i\in I_{g}
and any
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{\mathrm{Var}(A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}})}{n_{g}^{2}T}=\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\frac{\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}]\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}])}{n_{g}^{2}T}

\displaystyle=(2T)^{-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}]\right)^{2}-n_{g}^{-2}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}])^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=(2T)^{-1}\left(\left\|\Sigma_{1\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}-n_{g}^{-2}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}]\right\|^{2}\right),

which together with (
S730
) in the proof of Lemma
S69
implies that

\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1,i})=(2T)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\left\|\Sigma_{1\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}+O\left(\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right).

(S801)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{2,i})

\displaystyle=(2n^{2}T)^{-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]\right)^{2}-\sum_{i\leq n}(\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}])^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=(2T)^{-1}\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}w_{g}\Sigma_{2\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}+O\left(n^{-1}\right),

(S802)

and

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Cov}(\tilde{U}_{1,i},\tilde{U}_{2,i})

\displaystyle=2(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathrm{Cov}\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}A_{1,i}^{\top}A_{1,i^{\prime}},\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i-1}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=2(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i=N_{g}+2}^{N_{g}+n_{g}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=N_{g}+1}^{i-1}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i^{\prime}}A_{2,i^{\prime}}^{\top}]\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}])

\displaystyle=(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i^{\prime}}A_{1,i^{\prime}}^{\top}]-\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]\mathbb{E}[A_{2,i}A_{1,i}^{\top}]\right)

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}w_{g}\left\|\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}-(nT)^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{g}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[A_{1,i}A_{2,i}^{\top}]\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}w_{g}\left\|\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}+O\left(n^{-1}\right),

(S803)

where the last equality is by (
S753
) in the proof of
Lemma
S70
. Collecting the results in (
S801
), (
S802
) and (
S803
), we obtain

\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{1\gamma,i}^{\ast}-\tilde{U}_{2\gamma,i}^{\ast})=\frac{\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\left\|\Sigma_{1\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}-2w_{g}\left\|\Sigma_{12\gamma,g}\right\|^{2})+\left\|\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}w_{g}\Sigma_{2\gamma,g}\right\|^{2}}{2T}+O\left(\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}n_{g}^{-1}\right)

which establishes (
S787
).
Q.E.D.

Proof of Lemma
S74
. Recall that in the two
models investigated in this lemma, we have
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\{1,\ldots,n\}

and
\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{1\}
for model 1, and
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{1\}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{1,\ldots,T\}
for model 2. First notice that Assumption
10
(ii) holds with
G_{1}=n
,
M_{1}=1
,
G_{2}=1
and
M_{2}=T
. In
the proof of Lemma
S73
, we have shown that

\mathrm{Var}\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}\tilde{V}_{2,i}\right)=n^{-3}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}\left(T^{-1/2}A_{2,i}^{\top}A_{2,i}\right)\leq Kn^{-2}.

(S804)

Therefore, using the same arguments for deriving (
S800
) obtains

\frac{\omega_{n,T}^{2}}{(\omega_{n,T}^{\ast})^{2}}=1+O\left(n^{-1}\right),

and the claim in (
S788
) thus follows from Theorem
A3

and Theorem
A4
and Slutsky’s theorem.

By the independence of
z_{i,t}
across
i
for all
t

\mathrm{Var}\left(n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\leq n}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i}-\tilde{U}_{2,i})\right)=n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{\Psi}_{1,i}-\tilde{\Psi}_{2,i}+\tilde{V}_{1,i})+n^{-1}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{U}_{2,i}).

(S805)

The expression in (
S789
) is can be derived directly from (
S805
) and (
S802
) in the proof of Lemma
S73
.
Q.E.D.
